6. Answer to each new question to be started on a fresh page.
SET-2
Q.1(a) / 5 Marks / CO 2,3 / BL 2,3,4
Differentiate between self-concept and self-esteem. Explain how they influence an individual’s behaviour
and decision-making with appropriate examples.
Synoptic Answer
1. Conceptual Understanding:
(a) Self-Concept: Refers to how an individual perceives themselves, including beliefs about abilities,
roles, and identity. Answers: “Who am I?” (cognitive aspect)
(b) Self-Esteem: Refers to the value or worth an individual assigns to themselves. Reflects confidence
and self-acceptance; answers the question: “How do I feel about myself?” (emotional aspect)
2. Key Differences:
(a) Self-concept → knowledge and perception of self
(b) Self-esteem → evaluation and emotional judgment of self
Example: A person may believe they are weak in public speaking (self-concept) but still feel confident
overall (high self-esteem).
3. Influence on Behaviour and Decision-Making (Analysis):
(a) Self-Concept: Shapes perception of abilities; influences willingness to take risks and try new tasks.
(b) Self-esteem affects confidence, motivation, and emotional stability; high self-esteem leads to
better decisions and resilience; low self-esteem leads to fear, anxiety, and avoidance behaviour.
4. Conclusion: Self-concept and self-esteem together influence behaviour and decision-making. While
self-concept shapes how individuals view their capabilities, self-esteem determines how confidently they
act on them.
Q.1(b) / 5 Marks / CO 2 / BL 3
Define self-motivation and explain its key elements. How can an individual develop self-motivation in
challenging situations? Illustrate with suitable examples.
********** 1 **********
--- page 2 ---
Synoptic Answer
1. Definition of Self-Motivation: Self-motivation is the internal drive that enables individuals to take
initiative, pursue goals, and persist despite challenges without relying on external rewards.
2. Key Elements of Self-Motivation:
(a) Goal Setting: Provides direction and clarity
(b) Positive Attitude: Maintains optimism and confidence
(c) Self-Discipline: Ensures consistency in actions
(d) Persistence: Helps overcome obstacles and setbacks
3. Development in Challenging Situations (Application):
(a) Break Goals into Smaller Tasks: Reduces overwhelm and builds momentum
(b) Maintain a Positive Mindset: Focus on strengths and past achievements.
(c) Create Structured Plans: organise tasks with timelines and priorities.
(d) Track Progress: Regular monitoring enhances accountability.
(e) Reward Milestones: Reinforces motivation and consistency
(f) Minimise Distractions: Avoid procrastination and maintain focus.
Example: A student preparing for competitive exams can set daily targets, follow a study schedule, and
reward small achievements to stay motivated despite stress.
4. Conclusion: Self-motivation is essential for overcoming challenges and achieving goals. By developing
its key elements, individuals can enhance resilience, maintain focus, and improve performance in personal
and professional life.
Q.1(c) / 5 Marks / CO 2 / BL 2
Explain the concepts of adaptability and agility. Discuss how these qualities help individuals cope with
changing environments, with suitable examples.
Synoptic Answer
1. Conceptual Understanding:
(a) Adaptability: the ability to adjust thoughts, behaviours, and actions in response to change. Involves
flexibility, openness to learning, and acceptance of new situations
(b) Agility is the ability to respond quickly and effectively to change; it focuses on speed,
responsiveness, and proactive action.
2. Role in Coping with Change:
(a) Managing Uncertainty and Stress – Adaptability helps individuals accept change and remain flexible;
agility enables quick action, reducing delays and anxiety.
(b) Enhancing problem-solving: adaptable individuals explore alternatives; agile individuals implement
solutions efficiently.
(c) Supporting Learning and Growth – Adaptability encourages openness to feedback and new skills;
agility ensures rapid application of knowledge.
Example: In a workplace adopting new technology, adaptable employees learn new systems, while agile
employees quickly apply them and support others.
3. Conclusion: Adaptability and agility are essential for coping with dynamic environments. Together,
they enable individuals to remain flexible, responsive, and resilient, leading to improved performance and
continuous growth.
Q.1(d) / 5 Marks / CO 1 / BL 4
********** 2 **********
--- page 3 ---
Self-awareness is a continuous process of introspection and evaluation. Explain how SWOT analysis can
be used as a structured technique for self-assessment. Discuss its components and analyse how individuals
can align strengths with opportunities while minimizing weaknesses and threats for long-term growth.
Synoptic Answer
1. Concept of SWOT for Self-Awareness: SWOT analysis is a structured tool that evaluates strengths,
weaknesses (internal) and opportunities and threats (external), enabling continuous self-assessment and
informed decision-making.
2. Components of SWOT:
(a) Strengths: Skills, knowledge, positive traits (e.g., strong communication, discipline)
(b) Weaknesses: Limitations requiring improvement (e.g., poor time management)
(c) Opportunities: External growth avenues (e.g., training programs, networking)
(d) Threats: External challenges (e.g., competition, uncertainty)
3. Analysis for Personal Growth:
(a) Align Strengths with Opportunities: Use existing skills to leverage opportunities. Example: Strong
analytical ability + advanced courses → career advancement
(b) Minimise Weaknesses and Threats: Improve limitations through training and planning. Prepare for
external challenges proactively.
4. Benefits: Enhances self-awareness and strategic thinking; Supports goal-setting and better decision-
making; Promotes continuous learning and adaptability
5. Conclusion: SWOT analysis is an effective self-assessment tool that helps individuals align capabilities
with opportunities while managing risks, leading to sustained personal and professional growth.
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Q.2(a)(2)(i) / 5 Marks / CO 1 / BL 4
Explain the concept of time management. Analyse the importance of goal setting, organising, and
prioritizing tasks in achieving efficiency and productivity, with suitable examples.
Synoptic Answer
1. Concept of Time Management: Time management is the process of planning, organising, and
controlling time to accomplish tasks efficiently and achieve desired outcomes. It is essential for improving
productivity, meeting deadlines, and maintaining work-life balance.
2. Key Components and Their Importance (Analysis):
(a) Goal Setting provides direction and clarity; SMART goals (specific, measurable, achievable, relevant,
and time-bound) enhance focus. Example: Completing a syllabus within a fixed timeframe improves
consistency.
(b) Organising: - Involves structuring tasks, schedules, and resources systematically. Tools like to-do lists
and planners improve efficiency, reduce confusion and save time.
(c) Prioritisation – Identifies important and urgent tasks; ensures critical work is completed first. Tools
like the Eisenhower Matrix support decision-making.
3. Impact on Productivity: Enhances efficiency and reduces stress; improves decision-making and
performance. Example: An employee prioritizing key tasks meets deadlines effectively.
4. Conclusion: Effective time management, supported by goal setting, organising, and prioritisation,
enables individuals to optimise productivity, achieve goals, and maintain a balanced lifestyle.
Q.2(a)(2)(ii) / 5 Marks / CO 3 / BL 4
********** 3 **********
--- page 4 ---
Explain the concept of stress and analyse its impact on an individual’s physical, emotional, and
behavioural well-being. Discuss how effective stress management contributes to self-development and
proper conduct in life.
Synoptic Answer
1. Concept of Stress: Stress is a psychological and physiological response to demands or pressures when
perceived coping ability is insufficient. It may be eustress (positive, motivating) or distress (negative,
harmful).
2. Impact on Well-being (Analysis):
(a) Physical Impact – Headaches, fatigue, sleep disturbances, high blood pressure; long-term: weakened
immunity and health issues.
(b) Emotional Impact – Anxiety, irritability, mood swings, reduced confidence and motivation
(c) Behavioral Impact – Poor concentration, procrastination, withdrawal, or unhealthy habits (e.g.,
excessive screen time)
3. Role of Stress Management in Self-Development:
(a) Enhances Emotional Balance: Promotes calm and rational responses
(b) Improves Productivity: Better focus and time management.
(c) Builds Resilience: Enables coping with challenges effectively
(d) Supports Ethical Conduct: Reduces impulsive and negative behavior
Techniques: Mindfulness, exercise, time management, positive thinking
Example: A professional using mindfulness and planning can manage workload effectively and maintain
positive workplace relationships.
4. Conclusion: Effective stress management is essential for maintaining well-being, improving
performance, and ensuring balanced, ethical, and productive living.
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Q.2(b)(3)(i) / 5 Marks / CO 1 / BL 3, 4
Differentiate between sympathy and compassion. Analyse how compassion contributes to effective
leadership, with suitable examples.
Synoptic Answer
1. Conceptual Understanding:
(a) Sympathy involves feeling concern or sorrow for another person’s situation; it is passive in nature
and does not necessarily require action. Example: Expressing condolences to a colleague
(b) Compassion: Involves understanding others’ difficulties and taking action to help; Active and
supportive in nature; Example: Assisting a colleague in completing work during difficult times
2. Key Differences:
(a) Sympathy → emotional response without action
(b) Compassion → emotional understanding with supportive action
3. Role of Compassion in Leadership (Analysis):
(a) Builds Trust and Relationships: Leaders become approachable and reliable.
(b) Enhances Team Morale: Employees feel valued and supported
(c) Improves Communication: Encourages openness and collaboration
(d) Increases Motivation and Commitment: Employees respond positively to supportive leadership.
Example: A leader offering flexibility to an employee facing personal challenges demonstrates
compassion and builds long-term loyalty.
4. Conclusion: Compassion is a critical leadership quality that goes beyond sympathy by promoting action,
trust, and collaboration, thereby enhancing team performance and organizational effectiveness.
********** 4 **********
--- page 5 ---
Q.2(b)(3)(ii) / 5 Marks / CO 1 / BL 3
Define time management and explain its importance in achieving personal and professional goals, with
suitable examples.
Synoptic Answer
1. Definition of Time Management: Time management is the process of planning, organising, and
controlling time spent on activities to achieve maximum efficiency and productivity. It involves goal
setting, prioritizing tasks, and allocating appropriate time.
2. Importance in Achieving Goals:
(a) Improves Productivity and Efficiency – Ensures timely completion of tasks, reduces delays and
enhances performance
(b) Supports Goal Achievement – Aligns daily activities with short-term and long-term goals; helps
individuals stay focused and organised
(c) Maintains Work-Life Balance – Enables effective management of personal, academic, and professional
responsibilities; Reduces stress and burnout
(d) Enhances Decision-Making and Discipline – Encourages prioritisation of important tasks; minimises
procrastination and distractions.
Example: A student who plans a daily study schedule can balance academics and extracurricular activities
effectively, while an employee using task prioritisation can meet deadlines efficiently.
3. Conclusion: Effective time management is essential for achieving personal and professional goals. It
improves productivity, reduces stress, and enables individuals to lead a balanced and successful life.
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Q.2(c)(4)(i) / 5 Marks / CO 3 / BL 4
Analyse the role of active listening in improving decision-making effectiveness in organizations.
Synoptic Answer
1. Concept of Active Listening: Active listening is the process of fully concentrating, understanding, and
responding thoughtfully to a speaker. It involves attention, feedback, and empathy.
2. Role in Decision-Making (Analysis):
(a) Better Understanding of Information – Ensures accurate interpretation of ideas and viewpoints;
reduces chances of miscommunication.
(b) Reduction of Errors – Clarifies doubts through questioning and feedback. Prevents incorrect
assumptions and faulty decisions
(c) Enhances Collaboration – Encourages participation and inclusion of diverse perspectives, leading to
more informed and balanced decisions
(d) Improves Leadership Effectiveness – Builds trust and credibility; helps leaders make fair and well-
informed decisions.
Example: A manager who actively listens to team inputs can make better strategic decisions by
considering multiple viewpoints.
3. Conclusion: Active listening plays a critical role in decision-making by improving understanding,
reducing errors, and fostering collaboration, thereby enhancing overall leadership effectiveness and
organizational performance.
Q.2(c)(4)(ii) / 5 Marks / CO 1, 3 / BL 2, 3, 5
A conflict arises between two team members. Explain how interpersonal skills can be applied to resolve
the conflict effectively.
********** 5 **********
--- page 6 ---
Synoptic Answer
1. Context: Workplace conflicts can arise due to differences in opinions, communication gaps, or
misunderstandings. Effective interpersonal skills are essential for resolving such issues constructively.
2. Application of Interpersonal Skills:
(a) Active Listening – Listen attentively to both parties without interruption and understand perspectives
and underlying concerns.
(b) Neutral Facilitation – Act as an unbiased mediator; ensure fairness and equal opportunity for
expression.
(c) Empathy – Acknowledge emotions and viewpoints of both individuals; promote mutual understanding
and respect.
(d) Focus on Solution – Shift attention from blame to problem-solving. Encourage collaborative solutions
that benefit both parties.
3. Evaluation of Outcome: Reduces tension and misunderstandings; improves communication and
teamwork; builds trust and cooperation.
4. Conclusion: Applying interpersonal skills such as active listening, empathy, and neutral facilitation
helps resolve conflicts effectively, leading to stronger relationships and improved team performance.
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Q.2(d)(5)(i) / 5 Marks / CO 1, 2 / BL 3, 4, 5
Compare persuasion and manipulation. Analyse their key differences and evaluate their impact on trust
and outcomes in professional contexts.
Synoptic Answer
1. Conceptual Understanding:
(a) Persuasion: An ethical and transparent process of influencing others, based on logic, credibility,
and mutual benefit.
(b) Manipulation is a deceptive and self-serving approach to influence; it often involves misleading
information or hidden intent.
2. Key Differences (Analysis):
(a) Ethics: Persuasion → ethical and honest; Manipulation → unethical and deceptive
(b) Intent: Persuasion → mutual benefit; manipulation → personal gain
(c) Transparency: Persuasion → open communication; manipulation → hidden motives
3. Impact on Outcomes (Evaluation):
(a) Persuasion: Builds trust, credibility, and long-term relationships; encourages collaboration and
sustainable outcomes
(b) Manipulation: Leads to short-term gains; damages trust, relationships, and reputation.
For example, a leader can persuade others by explaining the benefits of a new policy, whereas
manipulation involves forcing acceptance through misleading information.
4. Conclusion: While persuasion fosters trust and ethical influence, manipulation may provide short-term
advantages but harms long-term relationships and organizational effectiveness.
Q.2(d)(5)(ii) / 5 Marks / CO 1, 2 / BL 3, 4, 5
Apply the Forced Connections technique to generate a new product idea. Explain the steps involved and
evaluate its effectiveness.
Synoptic Answer
1. Concept of Forced Connections: 'Forced connections' is a creative thinking technique that involves
combining unrelated ideas or objects to generate innovative solutions.
********** 6 **********
--- page 7 ---
2. Application Process:
(a) Combine Unrelated Concepts – Select two unrelated items and connect them. Example: Water bottle
+ reminder system
(b) Encourage Creative Thinking – Explore unconventional ideas without judgement; promote
brainstorming and imagination.
(c) Evaluate Feasibility – Assess practicality, usability, and market demand; identify potential benefits
and limitations.
(d) Develop Innovative Idea/Pitch – Refine the concept into a usable product. Example: A smart water
bottle that reminds users to stay hydrated
3. Evaluation of Effectiveness: Encourages out-of-the-box thinking; Breaks habitual patterns; enhances
innovation and problem-solving
4. Conclusion: The 'forced connections' technique is an effective tool for generating innovative ideas by
combining unrelated concepts, enabling individuals and teams to develop creative and practical solutions.
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Q.2(e)(6)(i) / 5 Marks / CO 1, 2 / BL 3, 4, 5
Evaluate the leadership of Ratan Tata or Satya Nadella in a value-driven context. Analyse how their
leadership reflects ethical principles and long-term impact.
Synoptic Answer
1. Concept of Value-Driven Leadership: Value-driven leadership focuses on aligning decisions and
actions with ethical principles, purpose, and stakeholder well-being.
2. Key Leadership Dimensions (Analysis):
(a) Ethical Governance – Emphasis on integrity, transparency, and responsible decision-making; builds
organisational credibility and trust.
(b) Stakeholder Focus – Balancing interests of employees, customers, society, and shareholders; Promotes
inclusive and sustainable growth
(c) Purpose-Driven Approach - Leadership guided by a larger mission beyond profit; Example: Social
initiatives and innovation-led growth
(d) Long-Term Impact – Focuses on sustainability, reputation, and organizational legacy; encourages
strategic and responsible leadership decisions
3. Evaluation: Strengthens trust and employee engagement; enhances brand value and organizational
culture; supports sustainable and ethical business practices.
4. Conclusion: The leadership of Ratan Tata and Satya Nadella exemplifies value-driven leadership by
integrating ethics, purpose, and stakeholder focus, leading to long-term organizational success and
societal impact.
Q.2(e)(6)(ii) / 5 Marks / CO 1, 2 / BL 3, 4, 5
Design an effective leadership response to an ethical crisis in a startup. Explain the key actions and
evaluate how they help in restoring trust and sustainability.
Synoptic Answer
1. Context: An ethical crisis (e.g., data misuse, misleading information) can damage credibility and
stakeholder trust. A structured leadership response is essential for recovery.
2. Key Elements of Leadership Response:
(a) Transparency – Acknowledge the issue openly and honestly; sharing accurate information with
stakeholders builds credibility and reduces uncertainty.
********** 7 **********
--- page 8 ---
(b) Stakeholder Communication – Engage with employees, customers, and investors. Provide timely
updates and address concerns. Demonstrates accountability and responsiveness
(c) Ethical Decision Framework – Assess the situation using ethical principles (fairness and
responsibility); Take corrective actions such as policy revision and compliance measures. Prevent
recurrence of similar issues
(d) Focus on Long-Term Trust – Prioritise sustainable reputation over short-term gains. Reinforce
organizational values and ethical culture.
3. Evaluation of Effectiveness: Restores stakeholder confidence, Strengthens organizational integrity;
enhances long-term sustainability
4. Conclusion: A transparent, ethical, and stakeholder-focused response enables startups to effectively
manage crises, rebuild trust, and ensure responsible long-term growth.
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Q.2(f)(7)(i) / 5 Marks / CO 2,3 / BL 6
A CEO must implement layoffs due to business constraints (as seen in many startups). Design how a
leader should demonstrate empathy and compassion during this process.
Synoptic Answer
1. Context: Layoffs are sensitive decisions that affect employee well-being and organizational reputation.
Compassionate leadership is essential to handle such situations responsibly.
2. Leadership Actions (Design):
(a) Transparent Communication – Clearly explain the reasons for layoffs; avoid ambiguity and
misinformation; maintain honesty and openness.
(b) Emotional Acknowledgement – Recognise employees’ contributions and emotions; Show empathy
through respectful dialogue; Provide a humane and supportive environment.
(c) Provide Support Mechanisms – Offer placement assistance, references, or career counselling. Provide
severance benefits where possible; Help employees transition smoothly
(d) Show Gratitude and Respect – Acknowledge efforts and contributions publicly; maintain dignity and
respect in the process.
3. Outcome (Evaluation): Preserves trust and organizational reputation; Reduces emotional distress;
strengthens employer brand and leadership credibility
4. Conclusion: Demonstrating empathy and compassion during layoffs ensures ethical leadership,
maintains trust, and reflects a humane approach to organizational challenges.
Q.2(f)(7)(ii) / 5 Marks / CO 1, 2, 3 / BL 3, 4, 5, 6
Explain and design a leadership blueprint for managing ethical AI-driven organizations. Discuss its
importance in ensuring responsible and sustainable outcomes.
Synoptic Answer
1. Concept and Importance: Ethical leadership in AI ensures that technologies are developed and used
with fairness, transparency, and accountability. Leaders must address challenges such as algorithmic bias,
privacy concerns, and societal impact.
2. Key Components of Leadership Blueprint:
(a) Define Core Values – Integrity, fairness, accountability, and inclusivity guide ethical decision-making.
(b) Ethical Governance – Establish policies for responsible AI use; monitor bias, data privacy, and
compliance.
(c) Transparency – Promote explainable AI systems and ensure clear communication with stakeholders.
********** 8 **********
--- page 9 ---
(d) Human-Centric Approach – Prioritise human well-being and ethical impact; Use AI to support, not
replace, human judgement.
(e) Continuous Learning - Update skills, policies, and ethical frameworks; adapt to evolving technologies
and risks.
3. Evaluation of Effectiveness: Builds stakeholder trust and credibility; Reduces ethical risks and misuse;
Supports sustainable innovation
4. Conclusion: A well-designed ethical AI leadership blueprint ensures responsible technology adoption,
balancing innovation with ethical considerations for long-term organizational and societal benefit.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
********** 9 **********
````

(c) Prioritisation – Identifies important and urgent tasks; ensures critical work is completed first. Tools

3. Impact on Productivity: Enhances efficiency and reduces stress; improves decision-making and
performance. Example: An employee prioritizing key tasks meets deadlines effectively.
4. Conclusion: Effective time management, supported by goal setting, organising, and prioritisation,
enables individuals to optimise productivity, achieve goals, and maintain a balanced lifestyle.
Q.2(a)(2)(ii) / 5 Marks / CO 3 / BL 4
********** 3 **********
--- page 4 ---
Explain the concept of stress and analyse its impact on an individual’s physical, emotional, and
behavioural well-being. Discuss how effective stress management contributes to self-development and
proper conduct in life.
Synoptic Answer
1. Concept of Stress: Stress is a psychological and physiological response to demands or pressures when
perceived coping ability is insufficient. It may be eustress (positive, motivating) or distress (negative,
harmful).
2. Impact on Well-being (Analysis):
(a) Physical Impact – Headaches, fatigue, sleep disturbances, high blood pressure; long-term: weakened
immunity and health issues.
(b) Emotional Impact – Anxiety, irritability, mood swings, reduced confidence and motivation
(c) Behavioral Impact – Poor concentration, procrastination, withdrawal, or unhealthy habits (e.g.,
excessive screen time)
3. Role of Stress Management in Self-Development:
(a) Enhances Emotional Balance: Promotes calm and rational responses
(b) Improves Productivity: Better focus and time management.
(c) Builds Resilience: Enables coping with challenges effectively
(d) Supports Ethical Conduct: Reduces impulsive and negative behavior
Techniques: Mindfulness, exercise, time management, positive thinking
Example: A professional using mindfulness and planning can manage workload effectively and maintain
positive workplace relationships.
4. Conclusion: Effective stress management is essential for maintaining well-being, improving
performance, and ensuring balanced, ethical, and productive living.
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Q.2(b)(3)(i) / 5 Marks / CO 1 / BL 3, 4
Differentiate between sympathy and compassion. Analyse how compassion contributes to effective
leadership, with suitable examples.
Synoptic Answer
1. Conceptual Understanding:
(a) Sympathy involves feeling concern or sorrow for another person’s situation; it is passive in nature
and does not necessarily require action. Example: Expressing condolences to a colleague
(b) Compassion: Involves understanding others’ difficulties and taking action to help; Active and
supportive in nature; Example: Assisting a colleague in completing work during difficult times
2. Key Differences:
(a) Sympathy → emotional response without action
(b) Compassion → emotional understanding with supportive action
3. Role of Compassion in Leadership (Analysis):
(a) Builds Trust and Relationships: Leaders become approachable and reliable.
(b) Enhances Team Morale: Employees feel valued and supported
(c) Improves Communication: Encourages openness and collaboration
(d) Increases Motivation and Commitment: Employees respond positively to supportive leadership.
Example: A leader offering flexibility to an employee facing personal challenges demonstrates
compassion and builds long-term loyalty.
4. Conclusion: Compassion is a critical leadership quality that goes beyond sympathy by promoting action,
trust, and collaboration, thereby enhancing team performance and organizational effectiveness.
********** 4 **********
--- page 5 ---
Q.2(b)(3)(ii) / 5 Marks / CO 1 / BL 3
Define time management and explain its importance in achieving personal and professional goals, with
suitable examples.
Synoptic Answer
1. Definition of Time Management: Time management is the process of planning, organising, and
controlling time spent on activities to achieve maximum efficiency and productivity. It involves goal
setting, prioritizing tasks, and allocating appropriate time.
2. Importance in Achieving Goals:
(a) Improves Productivity and Efficiency – Ensures timely completion of tasks, reduces delays and
enhances performance
(b) Supports Goal Achievement – Aligns daily activities with short-term and long-term goals; helps
individuals stay focused and organised
(c) Maintains Work-Life Balance – Enables effective management of personal, academic, and professional
responsibilities; Reduces stress and burnout
(d) Enhances Decision-Making and Discipline – Encourages prioritisation of important tasks; minimises
procrastination and distractions.
Example: A student who plans a daily study schedule can balance academics and extracurricular activities
effectively, while an employee using task prioritisation can meet deadlines efficiently.
3. Conclusion: Effective time management is essential for achieving personal and professional goals. It
improves productivity, reduces stress, and enables individuals to lead a balanced and successful life.
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Q.2(c)(4)(i) / 5 Marks / CO 3 / BL 4
Analyse the role of active listening in improving decision-making effectiveness in organizations.
Synoptic Answer
1. Concept of Active Listening: Active listening is the process of fully concentrating, understanding, and
responding thoughtfully to a speaker. It involves attention, feedback, and empathy.
2. Role in Decision-Making (Analysis):
(a) Better Understanding of Information – Ensures accurate interpretation of ideas and viewpoints;
reduces chances of miscommunication.
(b) Reduction of Errors – Clarifies doubts through questioning and feedback. Prevents incorrect
assumptions and faulty decisions
(c) Enhances Collaboration – Encourages participation and inclusion of diverse perspectives, leading to
more informed and balanced decisions
(d) Improves Leadership Effectiveness – Builds trust and credibility; helps leaders make fair and well-
informed decisions.
Example: A manager who actively listens to team inputs can make better strategic decisions by
considering multiple viewpoints.
3. Conclusion: Active listening plays a critical role in decision-making by improving understanding,
reducing errors, and fostering collaboration, thereby enhancing overall leadership effectiveness and
organizational performance.
Q.2(c)(4)(ii) / 5 Marks / CO 1, 3 / BL 2, 3, 5
A conflict arises between two team members. Explain how interpersonal skills can be applied to resolve
the conflict effectively.
********** 5 **********
--- page 6 ---
Synoptic Answer
1. Context: Workplace conflicts can arise due to differences in opinions, communication gaps, or
misunderstandings. Effective interpersonal skills are essential for resolving such issues constructively.
2. Application of Interpersonal Skills:
(a) Active Listening – Listen attentively to both parties without interruption and understand perspectives
and underlying concerns.
(b) Neutral Facilitation – Act as an unbiased mediator; ensure fairness and equal opportunity for
expression.
(c) Empathy – Acknowledge emotions and viewpoints of both individuals; promote mutual understanding
and respect.
(d) Focus on Solution – Shift attention from blame to problem-solving. Encourage collaborative solutions
that benefit both parties.
3. Evaluation of Outcome: Reduces tension and misunderstandings; improves communication and
teamwork; builds trust and cooperation.
4. Conclusion: Applying interpersonal skills such as active listening, empathy, and neutral facilitation
helps resolve conflicts effectively, leading to stronger relationships and improved team performance.
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Q.2(d)(5)(i) / 5 Marks / CO 1, 2 / BL 3, 4, 5
Compare persuasion and manipulation. Analyse their key differences and evaluate their impact on trust
and outcomes in professional contexts.
Synoptic Answer
1. Conceptual Understanding:
(a) Persuasion: An ethical and transparent process of influencing others, based on logic, credibility,
and mutual benefit.
(b) Manipulation is a deceptive and self-serving approach to influence; it often involves misleading
information or hidden intent.
2. Key Differences (Analysis):
(a) Ethics: Persuasion → ethical and honest; Manipulation → unethical and deceptive
(b) Intent: Persuasion → mutual benefit; manipulation → personal gain
(c) Transparency: Persuasion → open communication; manipulation → hidden motives
3. Impact on Outcomes (Evaluation):
(a) Persuasion: Builds trust, credibility, and long-term relationships; encourages collaboration and
sustainable outcomes
(b) Manipulation: Leads to short-term gains; damages trust, relationships, and reputation.
For example, a leader can persuade others by explaining the benefits of a new policy, whereas
manipulation involves forcing acceptance through misleading information.
4. Conclusion: While persuasion fosters trust and ethical influence, manipulation may provide short-term
advantages but harms long-term relationships and organizational effectiveness.
Q.2(d)(5)(ii) / 5 Marks / CO 1, 2 / BL 3, 4, 5
Apply the Forced Connections technique to generate a new product idea. Explain the steps involved and
evaluate its effectiveness.
Synoptic Answer
1. Concept of Forced Connections: 'Forced connections' is a creative thinking technique that involves
combining unrelated ideas or objects to generate innovative solutions.
********** 6 **********
--- page 7 ---
2. Application Process:
(a) Combine Unrelated Concepts – Select two unrelated items and connect them. Example: Water bottle
+ reminder system
(b) Encourage Creative Thinking – Explore unconventional ideas without judgement; promote
brainstorming and imagination.
(c) Evaluate Feasibility – Assess practicality, usability, and market demand; identify potential benefits
and limitations.
(d) Develop Innovative Idea/Pitch – Refine the concept into a usable product. Example: A smart water
bottle that reminds users to stay hydrated
3. Evaluation of Effectiveness: Encourages out-of-the-box thinking; Breaks habitual patterns; enhances
innovation and problem-solving
4. Conclusion: The 'forced connections' technique is an effective tool for generating innovative ideas by
combining unrelated concepts, enabling individuals and teams to develop creative and practical solutions.
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Q.2(e)(6)(i) / 5 Marks / CO 1, 2 / BL 3, 4, 5
Evaluate the leadership of Ratan Tata or Satya Nadella in a value-driven context. Analyse how their
leadership reflects ethical principles and long-term impact.
Synoptic Answer
1. Concept of Value-Driven Leadership: Value-driven leadership focuses on aligning decisions and
actions with ethical principles, purpose, and stakeholder well-being.
2. Key Leadership Dimensions (Analysis):
(a) Ethical Governance – Emphasis on integrity, transparency, and responsible decision-making; builds
organisational credibility and trust.
(b) Stakeholder Focus – Balancing interests of employees, customers, society, and shareholders; Promotes
inclusive and sustainable growth
(c) Purpose-Driven Approach - Leadership guided by a larger mission beyond profit; Example: Social
initiatives and innovation-led growth
(d) Long-Term Impact – Focuses on sustainability, reputation, and organizational legacy; encourages
strategic and responsible leadership decisions
3. Evaluation: Strengthens trust and employee engagement; enhances brand value and organizational
culture; supports sustainable and ethical business practices.
4. Conclusion: The leadership of Ratan Tata and Satya Nadella exemplifies value-driven leadership by
integrating ethics, purpose, and stakeholder focus, leading to long-term organizational success and
societal impact.
Q.2(e)(6)(ii) / 5 Marks / CO 1, 2 / BL 3, 4, 5
Design an effective leadership response to an ethical crisis in a startup. Explain the key actions and
evaluate how they help in restoring trust and sustainability.
Synoptic Answer
1. Context: An ethical crisis (e.g., data misuse, misleading information) can damage credibility and
stakeholder trust. A structured leadership response is essential for recovery.
2. Key Elements of Leadership Response:
(a) Transparency – Acknowledge the issue openly and honestly; sharing accurate information with
stakeholders builds credibility and reduces uncertainty.
********** 7 **********
--- page 8 ---
(b) Stakeholder Communication – Engage with employees, customers, and investors. Provide timely
updates and address concerns. Demonstrates accountability and responsiveness
(c) Ethical Decision Framework – Assess the situation using ethical principles (fairness and
responsibility); Take corrective actions such as policy revision and compliance measures. Prevent
recurrence of similar issues
(d) Focus on Long-Term Trust – Prioritise sustainable reputation over short-term gains. Reinforce
organizational values and ethical culture.
3. Evaluation of Effectiveness: Restores stakeholder confidence, Strengthens organizational integrity;
enhances long-term sustainability
4. Conclusion: A transparent, ethical, and stakeholder-focused response enables startups to effectively
manage crises, rebuild trust, and ensure responsible long-term growth.
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Q.2(f)(7)(i) / 5 Marks / CO 2,3 / BL 6
A CEO must implement layoffs due to business constraints (as seen in many startups). Design how a
leader should demonstrate empathy and compassion during this process.
Synoptic Answer
1. Context: Layoffs are sensitive decisions that affect employee well-being and organizational reputation.
Compassionate leadership is essential to handle such situations responsibly.
2. Leadership Actions (Design):
(a) Transparent Communication – Clearly explain the reasons for layoffs; avoid ambiguity and
misinformation; maintain honesty and openness.
(b) Emotional Acknowledgement – Recognise employees’ contributions and emotions; Show empathy
through respectful dialogue; Provide a humane and supportive environment.
(c) Provide Support Mechanisms – Offer placement assistance, references, or career counselling. Provide
severance benefits where possible; Help employees transition smoothly
(d) Show Gratitude and Respect – Acknowledge efforts and contributions publicly; maintain dignity and
respect in the process.
3. Outcome (Evaluation): Preserves trust and organizational reputation; Reduces emotional distress;
strengthens employer brand and leadership credibility
4. Conclusion: Demonstrating empathy and compassion during layoffs ensures ethical leadership,
maintains trust, and reflects a humane approach to organizational challenges.
Q.2(f)(7)(ii) / 5 Marks / CO 1, 2, 3 / BL 3, 4, 5, 6
Explain and design a leadership blueprint for managing ethical AI-driven organizations. Discuss its
importance in ensuring responsible and sustainable outcomes.
Synoptic Answer
1. Concept and Importance: Ethical leadership in AI ensures that technologies are developed and used
with fairness, transparency, and accountability. Leaders must address challenges such as algorithmic bias,
privacy concerns, and societal impact.
2. Key Components of Leadership Blueprint:
(a) Define Core Values – Integrity, fairness, accountability, and inclusivity guide ethical decision-making.
(b) Ethical Governance – Establish policies for responsible AI use; monitor bias, data privacy, and
compliance.
(c) Transparency – Promote explainable AI systems and ensure clear communication with stakeholders.
********** 8 **********
--- page 9 ---
(d) Human-Centric Approach – Prioritise human well-being and ethical impact; Use AI to support, not
replace, human judgement.
(e) Continuous Learning - Update skills, policies, and ethical frameworks; adapt to evolving technologies
and risks.
3. Evaluation of Effectiveness: Builds stakeholder trust and credibility; Reduces ethical risks and misuse;
Supports sustainable innovation
4. Conclusion: A well-designed ethical AI leadership blueprint ensures responsible technology adoption,
balancing innovation with ethical considerations for long-term organizational and societal benefit.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
********** 9 **********
````

SET-2
Q.1(a) / 5 Marks / CO 2,3 / BL 2,3,4
Differentiate between self-concept and self-esteem. Explain how they influence an individual’s behaviour
and decision-making with appropriate examples.
Synoptic Answer
1. Conceptual Understanding:
(a) Self-Concept: Refers to how an individual perceives themselves, including beliefs about abilities,
roles, and identity. Answers: “Who am I?” (cognitive aspect)
(b) Self-Esteem: Refers to the value or worth an individual assigns to themselves. Reflects confidence
and self-acceptance; answers the question: “How do I feel about myself?” (emotional aspect)
2. Key Differences:
(a) Self-concept → knowledge and perception of self
(b) Self-esteem → evaluation and emotional judgment of self
Example: A person may believe they are weak in public speaking (self-concept) but still feel confident
overall (high self-esteem).
3. Influence on Behaviour and Decision-Making (Analysis):
(a) Self-Concept: Shapes perception of abilities; influences willingness to take risks and try new tasks.
(b) Self-esteem affects confidence, motivation, and emotional stability; high self-esteem leads to
better decisions and resilience; low self-esteem leads to fear, anxiety, and avoidance behaviour.
4. Conclusion: Self-concept and self-esteem together influence behaviour and decision-making. While
self-concept shapes how individuals view their capabilities, self-esteem determines how confidently they
act on them.
Q.1(b) / 5 Marks / CO 2 / BL 3
Define self-motivation and explain its key elements. How can an individual develop self-motivation in
challenging situations? Illustrate with suitable examples.
********** 1 **********
--- page 2 ---
Synoptic Answer
1. Definition of Self-Motivation: Self-motivation is the internal drive that enables individuals to take
initiative, pursue goals, and persist despite challenges without relying on external rewards.
2. Key Elements of Self-Motivation:
(a) Goal Setting: Provides direction and clarity
(b) Positive Attitude: Maintains optimism and confidence
(c) Self-Discipline: Ensures consistency in actions
(d) Persistence: Helps overcome obstacles and setbacks
3. Development in Challenging Situations (Application):
(a) Break Goals into Smaller Tasks: Reduces overwhelm and builds momentum
(b) Maintain a Positive Mindset: Focus on strengths and past achievements.
(c) Create Structured Plans: organise tasks with timelines and priorities.
(d) Track Progress: Regular monitoring enhances accountability.
(e) Reward Milestones: Reinforces motivation and consistency
(f) Minimise Distractions: Avoid procrastination and maintain focus.
Example: A student preparing for competitive exams can set daily targets, follow a study schedule, and
reward small achievements to stay motivated despite stress.
4. Conclusion: Self-motivation is essential for overcoming challenges and achieving goals. By developing
its key elements, individuals can enhance resilience, maintain focus, and improve performance in personal
and professional life.
Q.1(c) / 5 Marks / CO 2 / BL 2
Explain the concepts of adaptability and agility. Discuss how these qualities help individuals cope with
changing environments, with suitable examples.
Synoptic Answer
1. Conceptual Understanding:
(a) Adaptability: the ability to adjust thoughts, behaviours, and actions in response to change. Involves
flexibility, openness to learning, and acceptance of new situations
(b) Agility is the ability to respond quickly and effectively to change; it focuses on speed,
responsiveness, and proactive action.
2. Role in Coping with Change:
(a) Managing Uncertainty and Stress – Adaptability helps individuals accept change and remain flexible;
agility enables quick action, reducing delays and anxiety.
(b) Enhancing problem-solving: adaptable individuals explore alternatives; agile individuals implement
solutions efficiently.
(c) Supporting Learning and Growth – Adaptability encourages openness to feedback and new skills;
agility ensures rapid application of knowledge.
Example: In a workplace adopting new technology, adaptable employees learn new systems, while agile
employees quickly apply them and support others.
3. Conclusion: Adaptability and agility are essential for coping with dynamic environments. Together,
they enable individuals to remain flexible, responsive, and resilient, leading to improved performance and
continuous growth.
Q.1(d) / 5 Marks / CO 1 / BL 4
********** 2 **********
--- page 3 ---
Self-awareness is a continuous process of introspection and evaluation. Explain how SWOT analysis can
be used as a structured technique for self-assessment. Discuss its components and analyse how individuals
can align strengths with opportunities while minimizing weaknesses and threats for long-term growth.
Synoptic Answer
1. Concept of SWOT for Self-Awareness: SWOT analysis is a structured tool that evaluates strengths,
weaknesses (internal) and opportunities and threats (external), enabling continuous self-assessment and
informed decision-making.
2. Components of SWOT:
(a) Strengths: Skills, knowledge, positive traits (e.g., strong communication, discipline)
(b) Weaknesses: Limitations requiring improvement (e.g., poor time management)
(c) Opportunities: External growth avenues (e.g., training programs, networking)
(d) Threats: External challenges (e.g., competition, uncertainty)
3. Analysis for Personal Growth:
(a) Align Strengths with Opportunities: Use existing skills to leverage opportunities. Example: Strong
analytical ability + advanced courses → career advancement
(b) Minimise Weaknesses and Threats: Improve limitations through training and planning. Prepare for
external challenges proactively.
4. Benefits: Enhances self-awareness and strategic thinking; Supports goal-setting and better decision-
making; Promotes continuous learning and adaptability
5. Conclusion: SWOT analysis is an effective self-assessment tool that helps individuals align capabilities
with opportunities while managing risks, leading to sustained personal and professional growth.
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Q.2(a)(2)(i) / 5 Marks / CO 1 / BL 4
Explain the concept of time management. Analyse the importance of goal setting, organising, and
prioritizing tasks in achieving efficiency and productivity, with suitable examples.
Synoptic Answer
1. Concept of Time Management: Time management is the process of planning, organising, and
controlling time to accomplish tasks efficiently and achieve desired outcomes. It is essential for improving
productivity, meeting deadlines, and maintaining work-life balance.
2. Key Components and Their Importance (Analysis):
(a) Goal Setting provides direction and clarity; SMART goals (specific, measurable, achievable, relevant,
and time-bound) enhance focus. Example: Completing a syllabus within a fixed timeframe improves
consistency.
(b) Organising: - Involves structuring tasks, schedules, and resources systematically. Tools like to-do lists
and planners improve efficiency, reduce confusion and save time.
(c) Prioritisation – Identifies important and urgent tasks; ensures critical work is completed first. Tools
like the Eisenhower Matrix support decision-making.
3. Impact on Productivity: Enhances efficiency and reduces stress; improves decision-making and
performance. Example: An employee prioritizing key tasks meets deadlines effectively.
4. Conclusion: Effective time management, supported by goal setting, organising, and prioritisation,
enables individuals to optimise productivity, achieve goals, and maintain a balanced lifestyle.

(a) Combine Unrelated Concepts – Select two unrelated items and connect them. Example: Water bottle
+ reminder system
(b) Encourage Creative Thinking – Explore unconventional ideas without judgement; promote
brainstorming and imagination.
(c) Evaluate Feasibility – Assess practicality, usability, and market demand; identify potential benefits
and limitations.
(d) Develop Innovative Idea/Pitch – Refine the concept into a usable product. Example: A smart water
bottle that reminds users to stay hydrated
3. Evaluation of Effectiveness: Encourages out-of-the-box thinking; Breaks habitual patterns; enhances
innovation and problem-solving
4. Conclusion: The 'forced connections' technique is an effective tool for generating innovative ideas by
combining unrelated concepts, enabling individuals and teams to develop creative and practical solutions.
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Q.2(e)(6)(i) / 5 Marks / CO 1, 2 / BL 3, 4, 5
Evaluate the leadership of Ratan Tata or Satya Nadella in a value-driven context. Analyse how their
leadership reflects ethical principles and long-term impact.
Synoptic Answer
1. Concept of Value-Driven Leadership: Value-driven leadership focuses on aligning decisions and
actions with ethical principles, purpose, and stakeholder well-being.
2. Key Leadership Dimensions (Analysis):
(a) Ethical Governance – Emphasis on integrity, transparency, and responsible decision-making; builds
organisational credibility and trust.
(b) Stakeholder Focus – Balancing interests of employees, customers, society, and shareholders; Promotes
inclusive and sustainable growth
(c) Purpose-Driven Approach - Leadership guided by a larger mission beyond profit; Example: Social
initiatives and innovation-led growth
(d) Long-Term Impact – Focuses on sustainability, reputation, and organizational legacy; encourages
strategic and responsible leadership decisions
3. Evaluation: Strengthens trust and employee engagement; enhances brand value and organizational
culture; supports sustainable and ethical business practices.
4. Conclusion: The leadership of Ratan Tata and Satya Nadella exemplifies value-driven leadership by
integrating ethics, purpose, and stakeholder focus, leading to long-term organizational success and
societal impact.
Q.2(e)(6)(ii) / 5 Marks / CO 1, 2 / BL 3, 4, 5
Design an effective leadership response to an ethical crisis in a startup. Explain the key actions and
evaluate how they help in restoring trust and sustainability.
Synoptic Answer
1. Context: An ethical crisis (e.g., data misuse, misleading information) can damage credibility and
stakeholder trust. A structured leadership response is essential for recovery.
2. Key Elements of Leadership Response:
(a) Transparency – Acknowledge the issue openly and honestly; sharing accurate information with
stakeholders builds credibility and reduces uncertainty.
********** 7 **********
--- page 8 ---
(b) Stakeholder Communication – Engage with employees, customers, and investors. Provide timely
updates and address concerns. Demonstrates accountability and responsiveness
(c) Ethical Decision Framework – Assess the situation using ethical principles (fairness and
responsibility); Take corrective actions such as policy revision and compliance measures. Prevent
recurrence of similar issues
(d) Focus on Long-Term Trust – Prioritise sustainable reputation over short-term gains. Reinforce
organizational values and ethical culture.
3. Evaluation of Effectiveness: Restores stakeholder confidence, Strengthens organizational integrity;
enhances long-term sustainability
4. Conclusion: A transparent, ethical, and stakeholder-focused response enables startups to effectively
manage crises, rebuild trust, and ensure responsible long-term growth.
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Q.2(f)(7)(i) / 5 Marks / CO 2,3 / BL 6
A CEO must implement layoffs due to business constraints (as seen in many startups). Design how a
leader should demonstrate empathy and compassion during this process.
Synoptic Answer
1. Context: Layoffs are sensitive decisions that affect employee well-being and organizational reputation.
Compassionate leadership is essential to handle such situations responsibly.
2. Leadership Actions (Design):
(a) Transparent Communication – Clearly explain the reasons for layoffs; avoid ambiguity and
misinformation; maintain honesty and openness.
(b) Emotional Acknowledgement – Recognise employees’ contributions and emotions; Show empathy
through respectful dialogue; Provide a humane and supportive environment.
(c) Provide Support Mechanisms – Offer placement assistance, references, or career counselling. Provide
severance benefits where possible; Help employees transition smoothly
(d) Show Gratitude and Respect – Acknowledge efforts and contributions publicly; maintain dignity and
respect in the process.
3. Outcome (Evaluation): Preserves trust and organizational reputation; Reduces emotional distress;
strengthens employer brand and leadership credibility
4. Conclusion: Demonstrating empathy and compassion during layoffs ensures ethical leadership,
maintains trust, and reflects a humane approach to organizational challenges.
Q.2(f)(7)(ii) / 5 Marks / CO 1, 2, 3 / BL 3, 4, 5, 6
Explain and design a leadership blueprint for managing ethical AI-driven organizations. Discuss its
importance in ensuring responsible and sustainable outcomes.
Synoptic Answer
1. Concept and Importance: Ethical leadership in AI ensures that technologies are developed and used
with fairness, transparency, and accountability. Leaders must address challenges such as algorithmic bias,
privacy concerns, and societal impact.
2. Key Components of Leadership Blueprint:
(a) Define Core Values – Integrity, fairness, accountability, and inclusivity guide ethical decision-making.
(b) Ethical Governance – Establish policies for responsible AI use; monitor bias, data privacy, and
compliance.
(c) Transparency – Promote explainable AI systems and ensure clear communication with stakeholders.
********** 8 **********
--- page 9 ---
(d) Human-Centric Approach – Prioritise human well-being and ethical impact; Use AI to support, not
replace, human judgement.
(e) Continuous Learning - Update skills, policies, and ethical frameworks; adapt to evolving technologies
and risks.
3. Evaluation of Effectiveness: Builds stakeholder trust and credibility; Reduces ethical risks and misuse;
Supports sustainable innovation
4. Conclusion: A well-designed ethical AI leadership blueprint ensures responsible technology adoption,
balancing innovation with ethical considerations for long-term organizational and societal benefit.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
********** 9 **********
````

````text
SYNOPTICS
SVKMs NMIMS
MUKESH PATEL SCH.OF TECH.MGMT ENGG-NM-M
Programme: B. Tech (AI)/B. Tech (AI & ML)/B. Tech (CIVIL)/B. Tech (COMP)/B. Tech
(CS)/B. Tech (CSE - CS)/B. Tech (DS)/B. Tech (EXTC)/B. Tech (IT)/B. Tech (MECH)/MBA

Year: III/Semester VI(Exam Year: 2025-2026)
Subject: Leading through Life Skills
Date: 16 May 2026   Time: 10:00 am to 01:00 pm (03:00 Hrs.)
Max. Marks: 100
FINAL EXAMINATION (Acad. Year:2025-2026)
Instructions:
1. This question paper contains 9 pages.
2. Candidates should read carefully the instructions.
3. Figure in right hand side indicates full marks.
4. Question No.1 is compulsory. Answer any four questions from the rest questionnaire.
5. In all 5 questions are to be attempted.
6. Answer to each new question to be started on a fresh page.
SET-2
Q.1(a) / 5 Marks / CO 2,3 / BL 2,3,4
Differentiate between self-concept and self-esteem. Explain how they influence an individual’s behaviour
and decision-making with appropriate examples.
Synoptic Answer
1. Conceptual Understanding:
(a) Self-Concept: Refers to how an individual perceives themselves, including beliefs about abilities,
roles, and identity. Answers: “Who am I?” (cognitive aspect)
(b) Self-Esteem: Refers to the value or worth an individual assigns to themselves. Reflects confidence
and self-acceptance; answers the question: “How do I feel about myself?” (emotional aspect)
2. Key Differences:
(a) Self-concept → knowledge and perception of self
(b) Self-esteem → evaluation and emotional judgment of self
Example: A person may believe they are weak in public speaking (self-concept) but still feel confident
overall (high self-esteem).
3. Influence on Behaviour and Decision-Making (Analysis):
(a) Self-Concept: Shapes perception of abilities; influences willingness to take risks and try new tasks.
(b) Self-esteem affects confidence, motivation, and emotional stability; high self-esteem leads to
better decisions and resilience; low self-esteem leads to fear, anxiety, and avoidance behaviour.
4. Conclusion: Self-concept and self-esteem together influence behaviour and decision-making. While
self-concept shapes how individuals view their capabilities, self-esteem determines how confidently they
act on them.
Q.1(b) / 5 Marks / CO 2 / BL 3
Define self-motivation and explain its key elements. How can an individual develop self-motivation in
challenging situations? Illustrate with suitable examples.
********** 1 **********
--- page 2 ---
Synoptic Answer
1. Definition of Self-Motivation: Self-motivation is the internal drive that enables individuals to take
initiative, pursue goals, and persist despite challenges without relying on external rewards.
2. Key Elements of Self-Motivation:
(a) Goal Setting: Provides direction and clarity
(b) Positive Attitude: Maintains optimism and confidence
(c) Self-Discipline: Ensures consistency in actions
(d) Persistence: Helps overcome obstacles and setbacks
3. Development in Challenging Situations (Application):
(a) Break Goals into Smaller Tasks: Reduces overwhelm and builds momentum
(b) Maintain a Positive Mindset: Focus on strengths and past achievements.
(c) Create Structured Plans: organise tasks with timelines and priorities.
(d) Track Progress: Regular monitoring enhances accountability.
(e) Reward Milestones: Reinforces motivation and consistency
(f) Minimise Distractions: Avoid procrastination and maintain focus.
Example: A student preparing for competitive exams can set daily targets, follow a study schedule, and
reward small achievements to stay motivated despite stress.
4. Conclusion: Self-motivation is essential for overcoming challenges and achieving goals. By developing
its key elements, individuals can enhance resilience, maintain focus, and improve performance in personal
and professional life.
Q.1(c) / 5 Marks / CO 2 / BL 2
Explain the concepts of adaptability and agility. Discuss how these qualities help individuals cope with
changing environments, with suitable examples.
Synoptic Answer
1. Conceptual Understanding:
(a) Adaptability: the ability to adjust thoughts, behaviours, and actions in response to change. Involves
flexibility, openness to learning, and acceptance of new situations
(b) Agility is the ability to respond quickly and effectively to change; it focuses on speed,
responsiveness, and proactive action.
2. Role in Coping with Change:
(a) Managing Uncertainty and Stress – Adaptability helps individuals accept change and remain flexible;
agility enables quick action, reducing delays and anxiety.
(b) Enhancing problem-solving: adaptable individuals explore alternatives; agile individuals implement
solutions efficiently.
(c) Supporting Learning and Growth – Adaptability encourages openness to feedback and new skills;
agility ensures rapid application of knowledge.
Example: In a workplace adopting new technology, adaptable employees learn new systems, while agile
employees quickly apply them and support others.
3. Conclusion: Adaptability and agility are essential for coping with dynamic environments. Together,
they enable individuals to remain flexible, responsive, and resilient, leading to improved performance and
continuous growth.
Q.1(d) / 5 Marks / CO 1 / BL 4
********** 2 **********
--- page 3 ---
Self-awareness is a continuous process of introspection and evaluation. Explain how SWOT analysis can
be used as a structured technique for self-assessment. Discuss its components and analyse how individuals
can align strengths with opportunities while minimizing weaknesses and threats for long-term growth.
Synoptic Answer
1. Concept of SWOT for Self-Awareness: SWOT analysis is a structured tool that evaluates strengths,
weaknesses (internal) and opportunities and threats (external), enabling continuous self-assessment and
informed decision-making.
2. Components of SWOT:
(a) Strengths: Skills, knowledge, positive traits (e.g., strong communication, discipline)
(b) Weaknesses: Limitations requiring improvement (e.g., poor time management)
(c) Opportunities: External growth avenues (e.g., training programs, networking)
(d) Threats: External challenges (e.g., competition, uncertainty)
3. Analysis for Personal Growth:
(a) Align Strengths with Opportunities: Use existing skills to leverage opportunities. Example: Strong
analytical ability + advanced courses → career advancement
(b) Minimise Weaknesses and Threats: Improve limitations through training and planning. Prepare for
external challenges proactively.
4. Benefits: Enhances self-awareness and strategic thinking; Supports goal-setting and better decision-
making; Promotes continuous learning and adaptability
5. Conclusion: SWOT analysis is an effective self-assessment tool that helps individuals align capabilities
with opportunities while managing risks, leading to sustained personal and professional growth.
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Q.2(a)(2)(i) / 5 Marks / CO 1 / BL 4
Explain the concept of time management. Analyse the importance of goal setting, organising, and
prioritizing tasks in achieving efficiency and productivity, with suitable examples.
Synoptic Answer
1. Concept of Time Management: Time management is the process of planning, organising, and
controlling time to accomplish tasks efficiently and achieve desired outcomes. It is essential for improving
productivity, meeting deadlines, and maintaining work-life balance.
2. Key Components and Their Importance (Analysis):
(a) Goal Setting provides direction and clarity; SMART goals (specific, measurable, achievable, relevant,
and time-bound) enhance focus. Example: Completing a syllabus within a fixed timeframe improves
consistency.
(b) Organising: - Involves structuring tasks, schedules, and resources systematically. Tools like to-do lists
and planners improve efficiency, reduce confusion and save time.
(c) Prioritisation – Identifies important and urgent tasks; ensures critical work is completed first. Tools
like the Eisenhower Matrix support decision-making.
3. Impact on Productivity: Enhances efficiency and reduces stress; improves decision-making and
performance. Example: An employee prioritizing key tasks meets deadlines effectively.
4. Conclusion: Effective time management, supported by goal setting, organising, and prioritisation,
enables individuals to optimise productivity, achieve goals, and maintain a balanced lifestyle.
Q.2(a)(2)(ii) / 5 Marks / CO 3 / BL 4
********** 3 **********
--- page 4 ---
Explain the concept of stress and analyse its impact on an individual’s physical, emotional, and
behavioural well-being. Discuss how effective stress management contributes to self-development and
proper conduct in life.
Synoptic Answer
1. Concept of Stress: Stress is a psychological and physiological response to demands or pressures when
perceived coping ability is insufficient. It may be eustress (positive, motivating) or distress (negative,
harmful).
2. Impact on Well-being (Analysis):
(a) Physical Impact – Headaches, fatigue, sleep disturbances, high blood pressure; long-term: weakened
immunity and health issues.
(b) Emotional Impact – Anxiety, irritability, mood swings, reduced confidence and motivation
(c) Behavioral Impact – Poor concentration, procrastination, withdrawal, or unhealthy habits (e.g.,
excessive screen time)
3. Role of Stress Management in Self-Development:
(a) Enhances Emotional Balance: Promotes calm and rational responses
(b) Improves Productivity: Better focus and time management.
(c) Builds Resilience: Enables coping with challenges effectively
(d) Supports Ethical Conduct: Reduces impulsive and negative behavior
Techniques: Mindfulness, exercise, time management, positive thinking
Example: A professional using mindfulness and planning can manage workload effectively and maintain
positive workplace relationships.
4. Conclusion: Effective stress management is essential for maintaining well-being, improving
performance, and ensuring balanced, ethical, and productive living.
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Q.2(b)(3)(i) / 5 Marks / CO 1 / BL 3, 4
Differentiate between sympathy and compassion. Analyse how compassion contributes to effective
leadership, with suitable examples.
Synoptic Answer
1. Conceptual Understanding:
(a) Sympathy involves feeling concern or sorrow for another person’s situation; it is passive in nature
and does not necessarily require action. Example: Expressing condolences to a colleague
(b) Compassion: Involves understanding others’ difficulties and taking action to help; Active and
supportive in nature; Example: Assisting a colleague in completing work during difficult times
2. Key Differences:
(a) Sympathy → emotional response without action
(b) Compassion → emotional understanding with supportive action
3. Role of Compassion in Leadership (Analysis):
(a) Builds Trust and Relationships: Leaders become approachable and reliable.
(b) Enhances Team Morale: Employees feel valued and supported
(c) Improves Communication: Encourages openness and collaboration
(d) Increases Motivation and Commitment: Employees respond positively to supportive leadership.
Example: A leader offering flexibility to an employee facing personal challenges demonstrates
compassion and builds long-term loyalty.
4. Conclusion: Compassion is a critical leadership quality that goes beyond sympathy by promoting action,
trust, and collaboration, thereby enhancing team performance and organizational effectiveness.
********** 4 **********
--- page 5 ---
Q.2(b)(3)(ii) / 5 Marks / CO 1 / BL 3
Define time management and explain its importance in achieving personal and professional goals, with
suitable examples.
Synoptic Answer
1. Definition of Time Management: Time management is the process of planning, organising, and
controlling time spent on activities to achieve maximum efficiency and productivity. It involves goal
setting, prioritizing tasks, and allocating appropriate time.
2. Importance in Achieving Goals:
(a) Improves Productivity and Efficiency – Ensures timely completion of tasks, reduces delays and
enhances performance
(b) Supports Goal Achievement – Aligns daily activities with short-term and long-term goals; helps
individuals stay focused and organised
(c) Maintains Work-Life Balance – Enables effective management of personal, academic, and professional
responsibilities; Reduces stress and burnout
(d) Enhances Decision-Making and Discipline – Encourages prioritisation of important tasks; minimises
procrastination and distractions.
Example: A student who plans a daily study schedule can balance academics and extracurricular activities
effectively, while an employee using task prioritisation can meet deadlines efficiently.
3. Conclusion: Effective time management is essential for achieving personal and professional goals. It
improves productivity, reduces stress, and enables individuals to lead a balanced and successful life.
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Q.2(c)(4)(i) / 5 Marks / CO 3 / BL 4
Analyse the role of active listening in improving decision-making effectiveness in organizations.
Synoptic Answer
1. Concept of Active Listening: Active listening is the process of fully concentrating, understanding, and
responding thoughtfully to a speaker. It involves attention, feedback, and empathy.
2. Role in Decision-Making (Analysis):
(a) Better Understanding of Information – Ensures accurate interpretation of ideas and viewpoints;
reduces chances of miscommunication.
(b) Reduction of Errors – Clarifies doubts through questioning and feedback. Prevents incorrect
assumptions and faulty decisions
(c) Enhances Collaboration – Encourages participation and inclusion of diverse perspectives, leading to
more informed and balanced decisions
(d) Improves Leadership Effectiveness – Builds trust and credibility; helps leaders make fair and well-
informed decisions.
Example: A manager who actively listens to team inputs can make better strategic decisions by
considering multiple viewpoints.
3. Conclusion: Active listening plays a critical role in decision-making by improving understanding,
reducing errors, and fostering collaboration, thereby enhancing overall leadership effectiveness and
organizational performance.
Q.2(c)(4)(ii) / 5 Marks / CO 1, 3 / BL 2, 3, 5
A conflict arises between two team members. Explain how interpersonal skills can be applied to resolve
the conflict effectively.
********** 5 **********
--- page 6 ---
Synoptic Answer
1. Context: Workplace conflicts can arise due to differences in opinions, communication gaps, or
misunderstandings. Effective interpersonal skills are essential for resolving such issues constructively.
2. Application of Interpersonal Skills:
(a) Active Listening – Listen attentively to both parties without interruption and understand perspectives
and underlying concerns.
(b) Neutral Facilitation – Act as an unbiased mediator; ensure fairness and equal opportunity for
expression.
(c) Empathy – Acknowledge emotions and viewpoints of both individuals; promote mutual understanding
and respect.
(d) Focus on Solution – Shift attention from blame to problem-solving. Encourage collaborative solutions
that benefit both parties.
3. Evaluation of Outcome: Reduces tension and misunderstandings; improves communication and
teamwork; builds trust and cooperation.
4. Conclusion: Applying interpersonal skills such as active listening, empathy, and neutral facilitation
helps resolve conflicts effectively, leading to stronger relationships and improved team performance.
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Q.2(d)(5)(i) / 5 Marks / CO 1, 2 / BL 3, 4, 5
Compare persuasion and manipulation. Analyse their key differences and evaluate their impact on trust
and outcomes in professional contexts.
Synoptic Answer
1. Conceptual Understanding:
(a) Persuasion: An ethical and transparent process of influencing others, based on logic, credibility,
and mutual benefit.
(b) Manipulation is a deceptive and self-serving approach to influence; it often involves misleading
information or hidden intent.
2. Key Differences (Analysis):
(a) Ethics: Persuasion → ethical and honest; Manipulation → unethical and deceptive
(b) Intent: Persuasion → mutual benefit; manipulation → personal gain
(c) Transparency: Persuasion → open communication; manipulation → hidden motives
3. Impact on Outcomes (Evaluation):
(a) Persuasion: Builds trust, credibility, and long-term relationships; encourages collaboration and
sustainable outcomes
(b) Manipulation: Leads to short-term gains; damages trust, relationships, and reputation.
For example, a leader can persuade others by explaining the benefits of a new policy, whereas
manipulation involves forcing acceptance through misleading information.
4. Conclusion: While persuasion fosters trust and ethical influence, manipulation may provide short-term
advantages but harms long-term relationships and organizational effectiveness.
Q.2(d)(5)(ii) / 5 Marks / CO 1, 2 / BL 3, 4, 5
Apply the Forced Connections technique to generate a new product idea. Explain the steps involved and
evaluate its effectiveness.
Synoptic Answer
1. Concept of Forced Connections: 'Forced connections' is a creative thinking technique that involves
combining unrelated ideas or objects to generate innovative solutions.
********** 6 **********
--- page 7 ---
2. Application Process:
(a) Combine Unrelated Concepts – Select two unrelated items and connect them. Example: Water bottle
+ reminder system
(b) Encourage Creative Thinking – Explore unconventional ideas without judgement; promote
brainstorming and imagination.
(c) Evaluate Feasibility – Assess practicality, usability, and market demand; identify potential benefits
and limitations.
(d) Develop Innovative Idea/Pitch – Refine the concept into a usable product. Example: A smart water
bottle that reminds users to stay hydrated
3. Evaluation of Effectiveness: Encourages out-of-the-box thinking; Breaks habitual patterns; enhances
innovation and problem-solving
4. Conclusion: The 'forced connections' technique is an effective tool for generating innovative ideas by
combining unrelated concepts, enabling individuals and teams to develop creative and practical solutions.
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
