````text
8. Encourage Collaboration and Innovation: Facilitate cross-functional teamwork and creative problem-
solving sessions.
9. Make Data-Informed Decisions: Use feedback and performance data to guide adjustments, ensuring
transparency and shared ownership of outcomes.
10. Promote a Positive Work Environment: Promote work-life balance, emotional intelligence, and
resilience-building activities.
Actionable Strategies to Achieve a Sustainable Work Environment:
- Implement Regular Team Check-ins to address issues and reinforce engagement.
-   Create Collaborative Platforms for knowledge sharing.
-   Offer Conflict Resolution Training to managers and team members.
- Recognize and Reward collaborative behaviors and achievements.
- Promote Work-Life Balance through flexible working arrangements.
- Encourage Personal Development initiatives aligned with organizational goals.
-   Maintain Transparent Policies to promote trust.
This integrated approach, rooted in foundational leadership principles, provides a structured pathway for
leaders to cultivate a positive, productive, and sustainable work environment, ultimately driving
organizational success.
********** 8 **********
````

## 2025-26 · Final-Exam · Sem 7 · Answer key · paper bddd7975e018

- Departments: Mechatronics
- Programmes: BTech Mechatronics 7004
- Source: `02_Question_Papers/2025/Sem_07/Final-Exam/BTech_Mechatronics_7004/Leading through Life Skills/ANS_Leading through Life Skills(702TG0I003)_2025-2026.pdf`
- Printed on paper: max marks: 100; duration hours: 3; exam date: 11 Dec 2025
- Text: text (8 pages)

````text
SYNOPTICS
SVKMs NMIMS
MUKESH PATEL SCH.OF TECH.MGMT ENGG-NM-M
Programme: B. Tech (AI)/B. Tech (AI & DS)/B. Tech (AI & ML)/B. Tech (CIVIL)/B. Tech
(COMP)/B. Tech (CS)/B. Tech (CSE - CS)/B. Tech (DS)/B. Tech (EXTC)/B. Tech Intg. (CE)/B.
Tech Intg. (DS)/B. Tech Intg. (IT)/B. Tech Intg. (Mech)/B. Tech (IT)/B. Tech (MECH)/B. Tech
(MXTC)/MBA Tech (AI)/MBA Tech (Comp)/MBA Tech (DS)/MBA Tech (IT)
Year: III / IV / V/Semester IX/Semester V/Semester VII(Exam Year: 2025-2026)
Subject: Leading through Life Skills
Date: 11 Dec 2025   Time: 02:00 pm to 05:00 pm (03:00 Hrs.)
Max. Marks: 100
FINAL EXAMINATION (Acad. Year:2025-2026)
Instructions:
1. This question paper contains 8 pages.
2. 1. Candidates should read carefully the instructions.
3. 2. Answer to each new question to be started on a fresh page.
4. 3. Figures in brackets against each question indicate full marks.
5. 4. Question No.1 is compulsory. Answer any four questions from the rest questionnaire.
1. Short notes/definitions/concepts (All questions compulsory) 20 Marks
I.   Define emotional agility and differentiate it from emotional intelligence with one example.
Synoptic Answer: Emotional agility = ability to navigate emotions adaptively; EI = recognition +
regulation; example: acknowledging fear before a presentation and acting confidently.
II.   Many people struggle with procrastination. Discuss the causes of procrastination and suggest time
management strategies to overcome it.
Synoptic Answer:
Procrastination is a common challenge where individuals delay important tasks despite knowing the
negative consequences. The causes of procrastination vary, but often include lack of motivation, fear of
failure, perfectionism, poor self-discipline, and distraction from activities like social media. Sometimes
tasks appear overwhelming, causing individuals to avoid them until the last moment. Low energy levels,
ineffective planning, and absence of clear goals can also make people postpone work.
To overcome procrastination, effective time management strategies are essential. Breaking large tasks
into smaller, manageable steps helps reduce the feeling of being overwhelmed. Prioritization tools, such
as the Eisenhower Matrix, can assist in distinguishing urgent and important tasks from less significant
ones. Creating a structured daily schedule with set deadlines encourages consistency. Using techniques
and methods, where work is done in focused intervals with short breaks, improves productivity.
Minimizing distractions, setting realistic goals, and rewarding progress further strengthen self-discipline.
By practicing these strategies, individuals can gradually replace procrastination with purposeful and
timely action.
III.   Explain the how Forced Connections can help in creative problem solving?
Synoptic Answer:
Forced Connections is a creative thinking technique that encourages people to connect unrelated ideas,
objects, or concepts to generate new insights or innovative solutions. It works on the principle that
********** 1 **********
--- page 2 ---
creativity often emerges from unexpected associations, when two seemingly unrelated things are
combined, they can produce a breakthrough idea. In short, it forces the mind to move beyond habitual
patterns of thinking.
It breaks mental fixedness, the tendency to think in predictable, habitual ways.
It stimulates cross-domain thinking, where solutions from one field inspire breakthroughs in another.
It expands associative networks in the brain, unlocking lateral thinking pathways.
It promotes collaborative ideation, since team members interpret connections differently.
IV. “Self-awareness is the foundation of ethical leadership.” Critically evaluate this statement by
integrating insights from Johari Window, Carl Rogers’ Self-Concept, and examples from Indian epics or
corporate leadership.
Synoptic Answer:
Discussed: Self-concept (real, ideal, perceived self); Johari Window’s open, blind, hidden, unknown
areas;
How feedback and self-disclosure foster authentic leadership; relate to Arjuna’s self-realization guided
by Krishna or Satya Nadella’s self-reflective leadership; Conclude on how self-awareness fosters trust,
ethics, and growth.
2. Case-based/scenario analysis & Essay/integrative reflection-application(Attempt Any 4),80
Marks
1.
(a) Case: During a campus project, conflicts arise due to unequal contributions. As the leader, how will
you apply dharma-based reasoning to restore harmony?
Synoptic Answer:
Define dharma (duty-based ethics); apply fairness, accountability, compassion; cite examples from
Ramayana (Rama’s adherence to duty); evaluate outcomes: justice, inclusion, and mutual respect.
(b) Explain how practicing adaptability and inner agility enables engineers to lead innovation in volatile
technological environments.
Synoptic Answer:
Define adaptability and agility; link to self-management; example; Airbnb pivot during COVID-19;
contrast rigidity vs growth mindset; outcome, innovation resilience.
2.
(a) Imagine you are a manager handling multiple projects at once. Some tasks are urgent, while others are
important but less time-sensitive. How will you use prioritization tools (like the Eisenhower Matrix) to
decide where to focus first?
Synoptic Answer:
As a manager handling multiple projects, prioritization is crucial to maintain efficiency and ensure critical
tasks are completed on time. I would use the Eisenhower Matrix to categorize tasks into four quadrants:
Urgent and Important (Do First): Tasks such as meeting client deadlines or resolving a sudden project
issue fall here. These require immediate attention, so I would allocate time and resources to handle them
first.
Important but Not Urgent (Schedule): Activities like strategic planning, skill development of team
members, or building client relationships are less time-sensitive but vital for long-term success. I would
schedule these into my calendar to ensure they are not neglected.
********** 2 **********
--- page 3 ---
Urgent but Not Important (Delegate): Tasks like routine reporting or administrative approvals, though
urgent, may not require my direct involvement. I would delegate them to capable team members, ensuring
they are done without consuming my focus.
Neither Urgent nor Important (Eliminate): Activities such as unnecessary meetings or low-value emails
would be minimized or eliminated to free up time.
By consistently applying this matrix, I can focus on high-impact activities, prevent burnout, and balance
short-term deliverables with long-term organizational goals.
(b) You are dealing with family expectations, career goals, and personal health issues simultaneously.
The stress is affecting your mood and relationships. How would you create a balanced lifestyle plan to
reduce stress, including time for rest, hobbies, and self-care?
Synoptic Answer:
Managing family expectations, career goals, and personal health issues simultaneously requires a balanced
lifestyle plan that addresses both responsibilities and well-being. First, I would start with self-awareness,
identifying stress triggers such as overcommitment, lack of rest, or unrealistic expectations. By clarifying
priorities, I can set realistic goals for career progress while maintaining family harmony and focusing on
health recovery.
I would design a structured daily routine that allocates specific time blocks for work, family interactions,
and self-care. Professional tasks would be scheduled during peak productivity hours, while family time
could be reserved for evenings or weekends to strengthen relationships. To protect my health, I would
include regular exercise, a balanced diet, and sufficient sleep in the plan, since physical well-being
enhances emotional resilience.
Equally important, I would dedicate time to rest and hobbies, such as reading, music, or outdoor activities,
to recharge mentally and emotionally. Practicing mindfulness or meditation would also help regulate
mood and reduce stress.
Finally, I would learn to set boundaries and delegate when necessary, ensuring I don’t shoulder every
responsibility alone. By balancing obligations with relaxation and personal growth, I can maintain better
relationships, improve my health, and pursue career goals effectively.
3.
(a) Read the following case and answer the questions mentioned below: Rohan is a senior sales executive
at EcoPrint Solutions, a company that sells sustainable printing systems to corporate offices. EcoPrint’s
flagship product, GreenEdge X5, uses recycled cartridges and consumes 40% less electricity than
conventional printers. The machine costs ₹2.5 lakh per unit, about 25% higher than regular printers. Rohan
has secured a meeting with NovaCorp, a mid-sized financial consultancy that prints thousands of pages
every week. The procurement head, Meera Sharma, is interested in exploring sustainable alternatives but
faces strict cost-cutting directives from management. NovaCorp’s current supplier has offered a lower
price and long-term service contract, making Meera hesitant to switch. Rohan must convince Meera that
GreenEdge X5 is not just an eco-friendly choice but a financially smart one. Meera, on the other hand,
wants solid evidence that the product will reduce long-term expenses and align with NovaCorp’s brand
image of responsible business practices. How can Rohan design and justify a persuasive sales strategy for
GreenEdge X5 by integrating and evaluating the three elements of Aristotle’s persuasion model,
credibility, emotion, and logic, to effectively influence Meera’s decision?
Synoptic Answer:
Rohan’s objective is to design a persuasive sales strategy that convinces Meera Sharma at NovaCorp to
approve a pilot purchase of the GreenEdge X5 printer, despite its higher price. To achieve this, he must
********** 3 **********
--- page 4 ---
combine credibility (Ethos), emotion (Pathos), and logic (Logos) into a coherent, ethical, and compelling
pitch that aligns with NovaCorp’s business and sustainability goals.
1. Establishing Credibility (Ethos)
Rohan should first position himself and EcoPrint Solutions as trustworthy and competent partners.
Professional credibility: Begin by briefly sharing EcoPrint’s track record—years in operation, key
corporate clients, and sustainability awards.
Personal credibility: Emphasize his technical expertise, customer success stories, and commitment to
post-sale service.
Ethical transparency: Acknowledge that the printer costs 25 percent more upfront but justify this honestly
as a long-term investment.
By demonstrating integrity, competence, and reliability, Rohan reduces perceived risk and builds Meera’s
confidence in both the product and the relationship.
2. Appealing to Emotion and Shared Values (Pathos)
To move beyond rational comparison, Rohan should connect the offer to NovaCorp’s identity and
aspirations.
Shared values: Link GreenEdge X5 to NovaCorp’s stated commitment to corporate responsibility and
sustainability reporting.
Vision narrative: Paint a picture of NovaCorp as a leader adopting green technologies that clients and
regulators will admire.
Empathy and relevance: Recognize Meera’s cost-cutting pressures and show understanding of her
accountability, positioning EcoPrint as a supportive partner, not a vendor. Visuals, images of reduced
carbon footprint, testimonials from employees proud of their firm’s green initiatives, can reinforce
emotional   engagement.
A well-crafted emotional connection transforms the proposal from a transaction into a partnership for a
cleaner, modern workplace.
3. Presenting Logical Evidence (Logos)
Rohan must provide concrete, data-driven proof that GreenEdge X5 is economically and operationally
superior.
Quantitative comparisons: Demonstrate 40 percent lower power consumption and 30 percent longer
cartridge life, translating these figures into annual cost savings.
ROI projection: Prepare a simple five-year cost-of-ownership chart showing payback within 18 months
due to energy and consumable savings.
Case study evidence: Present data from another client of similar scale where operational costs dropped
and employee satisfaction rose.
Logical clarity reinforces that sustainability and profitability can coexist, satisfying Meera’s need for
measurable justification.
(b) Case Study: A mid-level manager is tasked with improving team performance and workplace culture,
but some team members are skeptical about adopting new leadership practices.
Evaluate how Spiritual Leadership can help leaders inspire ethical behavior, motivate teams, and create a long-
term positive organizational culture.
Synoptic Answer:
Spiritual Leadership can significantly help a mid-level manager improve team performance and workplace
culture by inspiring ethical behavior, motivating teams intrinsically, and creating a long-term positive
organizational culture. It does so by fostering a sense of meaning, purpose, and connection to a shared
vision within the team, while emphasizing core spiritual values such as altruistic love, integrity, honesty,
and care for others.
********** 4 **********
--- page 5 ---
Inspiring Ethical Behavior -Spiritual leadership emphasizes ethical standards like transparency, honesty,
integrity, and commitment as foundational elements. This encourages team members to align their
personal and professional values with the organization’s ethical norms, reducing skepticism about new
leadership practices and promoting trust among team members.
Motivating Teams - Intrinsic motivation leads to better task performance, knowledge sharing, and
creativity.
Creating Long-term Positive Culture - Spiritual leadership promotes a workplace culture grounded in
shared vision, hope, faith, and altruistic love. This culture supports employees’ well-being, reduces stress
and emotional fatigue, and nurtures psychological safety. Over time, this leads to higher job satisfaction,
lower turnover, and sustained commitment to the organization’s mission. Employees feel valued,
connected, and driven by purpose, which enhances loyalty and meaningful contributions, ultimately
producing a resilient, positive organizational culture.
Practical Steps for the Manager - Communicate a clear, inspiring vision that connects team members to
a larger purpose beyond daily tasks. Model and reinforce ethical behaviors consistently. Show authentic
care for team members’ growth and well-being. Encourage open communication and psychological safety
to include all voices. Promote a supportive community that shares spiritual values aligned with
organizational goals.
Thus, spiritual leadership offers a powerful framework for the manager to transform team dynamics
ethically and motivationally while building a cohesive long-term culture.
4.   Ritika, a project manager, must decide whether to launch a new product prototype that passed
testing but still faces market uncertainty. Her team is divided, some see opportunity, others warn of risk.
How can Ritika apply all six thinking hats, white, red, black, yellow, green, and blue; to evaluate facts,
emotions, risks, opportunities, creativity, and process control for making a well-balanced decision?
Synoptic Answer:
Ritika can use all six thinking hats to make a balanced and well-informed decision.
Under the White Hat, she should focus on facts and data, market trends, testing results, and competitor
insights.
The Red Hat lets her and her team express gut feelings or anxieties about the launch honestly.
Using the Black Hat, she evaluates possible risks such as poor market response, high costs, or supply
delays.
The Yellow Hat helps her identify potential benefits like brand visibility, innovation leadership, and
customer excitement.
With the Green Hat, she can brainstorm creative strategies, such as limited trials or collaborations, to
reduce risk while testing the market.
Finally, the Blue Hat guides the overall process, organizing discussions, summarizing viewpoints, and
helping the team reach a clear, structured decision.
5.   Riya is a marketing executive at a growing IT firm. Although she is technically competent and meets
her targets, she faces challenges in building strong relationships with her colleagues and clients. During
team meetings, she often hesitates to share her ideas, fearing criticism, while some team members
dominate discussions. Recently, a client feedback session revealed that Riya struggled to understand the
client’s perspective, leading to miscommunication and a minor project delay. Her manager notices that
while Riya excels in technical tasks, her limited interpersonal skills, such as communication, active
listening, empathy, and teamwork, affect collaboration and client satisfaction. Riya wants to improve her
interpersonal effectiveness to strengthen professional relationships, contribute more actively in meetings,
********** 5 **********
--- page 6 ---
and handle conflicts or misunderstandings gracefully. She seeks guidance on practical strategies to
enhance her skills in real workplace situations.
(a) Identify the key interpersonal skill challenges faced by Riya and explain how they impact her
professional growth.
(b) Suggest at least three practical strategies Riya can use to improve communication and active listening
within her team.
(c) How can empathy and cultural sensitivity help Riya handle client interactions more effectively?
(d) Imagine you are Riya’s mentor. Design a short action plan to enhance her teamwork, assertiveness,
and conflict-resolution skills.
Synoptic Answer:
(a) Identify the key interpersonal skill challenges faced by Riya and explain how they impact her
professional growth. - Riya’s primary interpersonal challenges include hesitation to express ideas, limited
active listening, low assertiveness, and insufficient empathy. Hesitation in sharing ideas during meetings
prevents her from contributing valuable insights, reducing her visibility and influence in team discussions.
Limited active listening and understanding of colleagues’ and clients’ perspectives lead to
miscommunication, project delays, and diminished collaboration. Low assertiveness means she struggles
to voice concerns or negotiate effectively, impacting her ability to lead or manage responsibilities
confidently. Insufficient empathy affects her client interactions and teamwork, as she may fail to
recognize others’ needs or emotions, lowering trust and rapport. Collectively, these gaps hinder her
professional growth, as interpersonal effectiveness is crucial for leadership opportunities, team cohesion,
client satisfaction, and career advancement. Addressing these challenges is essential for Riya to excel
beyond technical competence and become a well-rounded professional.
(b) Suggest at least three practical strategies Riya can use to improve communication and active listening
within her team - Riya can improve communication and active listening using several strategies. First, she
should practice structured participation in meetings, preparing points in advance and gradually
contributing ideas to build confidence. Second, she should adopt active listening techniques, such as
maintaining eye contact, nodding, summarizing key points, and asking clarifying questions, which
demonstrates engagement and reduces misunderstandings. Third, Riya can seek feedback from colleagues
after discussions to understand how her communication is perceived and identify areas for improvement.
Additionally, using collaborative tools for idea-sharing can ensure her input is visible even if she hesitates
to speak in large meetings. Regular reflection on interactions, journaling communication challenges, and
observing effective communicators within her organization can further enhance her skills. Implementing
these strategies consistently will help Riya express herself confidently, understand team dynamics better,
and strengthen professional relationships.
(c) How can empathy and cultural sensitivity help Riya handle client interactions more effectively? -
Empathy allows Riya to understand clients’ perspectives, emotions, and expectations, enabling her to
respond appropriately and build trust. By actively listening and validating clients’ concerns, she can foster
a positive rapport and ensure solutions align with their needs. Cultural sensitivity further enhances
interactions, especially in a diverse client base. Being aware of cultural norms, communication styles, and
values helps Riya avoid misunderstandings, demonstrate respect, and adapt her approach accordingly. For
example, understanding a client’s preference for formal communication or decision-making processes can
prevent friction and miscommunication. By combining empathy with cultural awareness, Riya can
anticipate client concerns, address issues proactively, and strengthen long-term relationships, enhancing
client satisfaction and project outcomes. These skills not only improve professional credibility but also
contribute to her growth as a versatile, globally competent professional.
********** 6 **********
--- page 7 ---
(d) Imagine you are Riya’s mentor. Design a short action plan to enhance her teamwork, assertiveness,
and conflict-resolution skills. As Riya’s mentor, I would create a three-step action plan. First, to improve
teamwork, I would encourage her to actively participate in small group discussions, volunteer for
collaborative tasks, and acknowledge colleagues’ contributions to build mutual trust. Second, to enhance
assertiveness, she should practice expressing opinions clearly in low-stakes settings, gradually increasing
participation in larger meetings, and use “I” statements to communicate ideas confidently without
appearing aggressive. Third, for conflict resolution, I would teach her to listen empathetically, identify
the root cause of disagreements, and facilitate compromise by proposing solutions that respect all
perspectives. Additional steps include role-playing scenarios, seeking constructive feedback, and
reflecting on experiences to identify improvements. Regular check-ins would track progress, ensuring the
plan translates into consistent behavior changes. Implementing this action plan will strengthen Riya’s
interpersonal effectiveness, enabling her to collaborate, lead, and handle workplace challenges gracefully.
6.   Discuss the principles of effective leadership in promoting team collaboration, motivation, and engagement.
(a) Create a comprehensive plan showing how a leader can apply these principles to resolve conflicts, address
disengagement, and improve coordination within teams.
Synoptic Answer:
(a) Principles of Effective Leadership in promoting team collaboration, motivation, and engagement
revolve around setting clear goals, fostering open communication, building trust, and nurturing a positive
environment. These principles help in resolving conflicts, addressing disengagement, and improving
coordination within teams. Core principles include:
Visibility and Availability: Leaders being accessible encourages open dialogue and support.
Effective Communication: Clarity and transparency ensure everyone understands their roles and the team's
objectives.
Trust and Respect: Building trust through consistency and integrity promotes a safe environment for
sharing ideas.
Shared Vision and Goals: Aligning team efforts with common objectives increases motivation.
Empathy and Inclusivity: Recognizing individual differences promotes engagement.
Recognition and Feedback: Regular acknowledgment and constructive feedback boost morale.
Encouraging Innovation: Creating space for creativity energizes teams.
(b) Suggest actionable strategies to develop a positive, productive, and sustainable work environment.
Synoptic Answer:
(b) Comprehensive Plan for Leadership Application:
1. Establish Clear, SMART Goals: Set specific, measurable, attainable, relevant, and time-bound
objectives to guide team efforts and provide clarity.
2. Promote Open Communication: Develop channels for transparent information sharing team meetings,
digital platforms and promote a culture where questions and feedback are welcomed.
3. Promote Trust and Psychological Safety: Lead by example with honesty, integrity, and reliability.
Address conflicts proactively by listening actively to all parties involved.
4. Provide Vision and Purpose: Communicate a compelling vision that aligns with team values, inspiring
intrinsic motivation.
5. Build Inclusive and Respectful Culture: Encourage diversity, active listening, and mutual respect,
ensuring all voices are heard.
6. Recognize Contributions Regularly: Celebrate achievements and provide recognition tailored to
individual and team efforts.
********** 7 **********
--- page 8 ---
7. Develop and Empower Team Members: Invest in ongoing training, mentorship, and personal
development initiatives.
8. Encourage Collaboration and Innovation: Facilitate cross-functional teamwork and creative problem-
solving sessions.
9. Make Data-Informed Decisions: Use feedback and performance data to guide adjustments, ensuring
transparency and shared ownership of outcomes.
10. Promote a Positive Work Environment: Promote work-life balance, emotional intelligence, and
resilience-building activities.
Actionable Strategies to Achieve a Sustainable Work Environment:
- Implement Regular Team Check-ins to address issues and reinforce engagement.
-   Create Collaborative Platforms for knowledge sharing.
-   Offer Conflict Resolution Training to managers and team members.
- Recognize and Reward collaborative behaviors and achievements.
- Promote Work-Life Balance through flexible working arrangements.
- Encourage Personal Development initiatives aligned with organizational goals.
-   Maintain Transparent Policies to promote trust.
This integrated approach, rooted in foundational leadership principles, provides a structured pathway for
leaders to cultivate a positive, productive, and sustainable work environment, ultimately driving
organizational success.
********** 8 **********
````

````text
and self-care. Professional tasks would be scheduled during peak productivity hours, while family time
could be reserved for evenings or weekends to strengthen relationships. To protect my health, I would
include regular exercise, a balanced diet, and sufficient sleep in the plan, since physical well-being
enhances emotional resilience.
Equally important, I would dedicate time to rest and hobbies, such as reading, music, or outdoor activities,
to recharge mentally and emotionally. Practicing mindfulness or meditation would also help regulate
mood and reduce stress.
Finally, I would learn to set boundaries and delegate when necessary, ensuring I don’t shoulder every
responsibility alone. By balancing obligations with relaxation and personal growth, I can maintain better
relationships, improve my health, and pursue career goals effectively.
3.
(a) Read the following case and answer the questions mentioned below: Rohan is a senior sales executive
at EcoPrint Solutions, a company that sells sustainable printing systems to corporate offices. EcoPrint’s
flagship product, GreenEdge X5, uses recycled cartridges and consumes 40% less electricity than
conventional printers. The machine costs ₹2.5 lakh per unit, about 25% higher than regular printers. Rohan
has secured a meeting with NovaCorp, a mid-sized financial consultancy that prints thousands of pages
every week. The procurement head, Meera Sharma, is interested in exploring sustainable alternatives but
faces strict cost-cutting directives from management. NovaCorp’s current supplier has offered a lower
price and long-term service contract, making Meera hesitant to switch. Rohan must convince Meera that
GreenEdge X5 is not just an eco-friendly choice but a financially smart one. Meera, on the other hand,
wants solid evidence that the product will reduce long-term expenses and align with NovaCorp’s brand
image of responsible business practices. How can Rohan design and justify a persuasive sales strategy for
GreenEdge X5 by integrating and evaluating the three elements of Aristotle’s persuasion model,
credibility, emotion, and logic, to effectively influence Meera’s decision?
Synoptic Answer:
Rohan’s objective is to design a persuasive sales strategy that convinces Meera Sharma at NovaCorp to
approve a pilot purchase of the GreenEdge X5 printer, despite its higher price. To achieve this, he must
********** 3 **********
--- page 4 ---
combine credibility (Ethos), emotion (Pathos), and logic (Logos) into a coherent, ethical, and compelling
pitch that aligns with NovaCorp’s business and sustainability goals.
1. Establishing Credibility (Ethos)
Rohan should first position himself and EcoPrint Solutions as trustworthy and competent partners.
Professional credibility: Begin by briefly sharing EcoPrint’s track record—years in operation, key
corporate clients, and sustainability awards.
Personal credibility: Emphasize his technical expertise, customer success stories, and commitment to
post-sale service.
Ethical transparency: Acknowledge that the printer costs 25 percent more upfront but justify this honestly
as a long-term investment.
By demonstrating integrity, competence, and reliability, Rohan reduces perceived risk and builds Meera’s
confidence in both the product and the relationship.
2. Appealing to Emotion and Shared Values (Pathos)
To move beyond rational comparison, Rohan should connect the offer to NovaCorp’s identity and
aspirations.
Shared values: Link GreenEdge X5 to NovaCorp’s stated commitment to corporate responsibility and
sustainability reporting.
Vision narrative: Paint a picture of NovaCorp as a leader adopting green technologies that clients and
regulators will admire.
Empathy and relevance: Recognize Meera’s cost-cutting pressures and show understanding of her
accountability, positioning EcoPrint as a supportive partner, not a vendor. Visuals, images of reduced
carbon footprint, testimonials from employees proud of their firm’s green initiatives, can reinforce
emotional   engagement.
A well-crafted emotional connection transforms the proposal from a transaction into a partnership for a
cleaner, modern workplace.
3. Presenting Logical Evidence (Logos)
Rohan must provide concrete, data-driven proof that GreenEdge X5 is economically and operationally
superior.
Quantitative comparisons: Demonstrate 40 percent lower power consumption and 30 percent longer
cartridge life, translating these figures into annual cost savings.
ROI projection: Prepare a simple five-year cost-of-ownership chart showing payback within 18 months
due to energy and consumable savings.
Case study evidence: Present data from another client of similar scale where operational costs dropped
and employee satisfaction rose.
Logical clarity reinforces that sustainability and profitability can coexist, satisfying Meera’s need for
measurable justification.
(b) Case Study: A mid-level manager is tasked with improving team performance and workplace culture,
but some team members are skeptical about adopting new leadership practices.
Evaluate how Spiritual Leadership can help leaders inspire ethical behavior, motivate teams, and create a long-
term positive organizational culture.
Synoptic Answer:
Spiritual Leadership can significantly help a mid-level manager improve team performance and workplace
culture by inspiring ethical behavior, motivating teams intrinsically, and creating a long-term positive
organizational culture. It does so by fostering a sense of meaning, purpose, and connection to a shared
vision within the team, while emphasizing core spiritual values such as altruistic love, integrity, honesty,
and care for others.
********** 4 **********
--- page 5 ---
Inspiring Ethical Behavior -Spiritual leadership emphasizes ethical standards like transparency, honesty,
integrity, and commitment as foundational elements. This encourages team members to align their
personal and professional values with the organization’s ethical norms, reducing skepticism about new
leadership practices and promoting trust among team members.
Motivating Teams - Intrinsic motivation leads to better task performance, knowledge sharing, and
creativity.
Creating Long-term Positive Culture - Spiritual leadership promotes a workplace culture grounded in
shared vision, hope, faith, and altruistic love. This culture supports employees’ well-being, reduces stress
and emotional fatigue, and nurtures psychological safety. Over time, this leads to higher job satisfaction,
lower turnover, and sustained commitment to the organization’s mission. Employees feel valued,
connected, and driven by purpose, which enhances loyalty and meaningful contributions, ultimately
producing a resilient, positive organizational culture.
Practical Steps for the Manager - Communicate a clear, inspiring vision that connects team members to
a larger purpose beyond daily tasks. Model and reinforce ethical behaviors consistently. Show authentic
care for team members’ growth and well-being. Encourage open communication and psychological safety
to include all voices. Promote a supportive community that shares spiritual values aligned with
organizational goals.
Thus, spiritual leadership offers a powerful framework for the manager to transform team dynamics
ethically and motivationally while building a cohesive long-term culture.
4.   Ritika, a project manager, must decide whether to launch a new product prototype that passed
testing but still faces market uncertainty. Her team is divided, some see opportunity, others warn of risk.
How can Ritika apply all six thinking hats, white, red, black, yellow, green, and blue; to evaluate facts,
emotions, risks, opportunities, creativity, and process control for making a well-balanced decision?
Synoptic Answer:
Ritika can use all six thinking hats to make a balanced and well-informed decision.
Under the White Hat, she should focus on facts and data, market trends, testing results, and competitor
insights.
The Red Hat lets her and her team express gut feelings or anxieties about the launch honestly.
Using the Black Hat, she evaluates possible risks such as poor market response, high costs, or supply
delays.
The Yellow Hat helps her identify potential benefits like brand visibility, innovation leadership, and
customer excitement.
With the Green Hat, she can brainstorm creative strategies, such as limited trials or collaborations, to
reduce risk while testing the market.
Finally, the Blue Hat guides the overall process, organizing discussions, summarizing viewpoints, and
helping the team reach a clear, structured decision.
5.   Riya is a marketing executive at a growing IT firm. Although she is technically competent and meets
her targets, she faces challenges in building strong relationships with her colleagues and clients. During
team meetings, she often hesitates to share her ideas, fearing criticism, while some team members
dominate discussions. Recently, a client feedback session revealed that Riya struggled to understand the
client’s perspective, leading to miscommunication and a minor project delay. Her manager notices that
while Riya excels in technical tasks, her limited interpersonal skills, such as communication, active
listening, empathy, and teamwork, affect collaboration and client satisfaction. Riya wants to improve her
interpersonal effectiveness to strengthen professional relationships, contribute more actively in meetings,
********** 5 **********
--- page 6 ---
and handle conflicts or misunderstandings gracefully. She seeks guidance on practical strategies to
enhance her skills in real workplace situations.
(a) Identify the key interpersonal skill challenges faced by Riya and explain how they impact her
professional growth.
(b) Suggest at least three practical strategies Riya can use to improve communication and active listening
within her team.
(c) How can empathy and cultural sensitivity help Riya handle client interactions more effectively?
(d) Imagine you are Riya’s mentor. Design a short action plan to enhance her teamwork, assertiveness,
and conflict-resolution skills.
Synoptic Answer:
(a) Identify the key interpersonal skill challenges faced by Riya and explain how they impact her
professional growth. - Riya’s primary interpersonal challenges include hesitation to express ideas, limited
active listening, low assertiveness, and insufficient empathy. Hesitation in sharing ideas during meetings
prevents her from contributing valuable insights, reducing her visibility and influence in team discussions.
Limited active listening and understanding of colleagues’ and clients’ perspectives lead to
miscommunication, project delays, and diminished collaboration. Low assertiveness means she struggles
to voice concerns or negotiate effectively, impacting her ability to lead or manage responsibilities
confidently. Insufficient empathy affects her client interactions and teamwork, as she may fail to
recognize others’ needs or emotions, lowering trust and rapport. Collectively, these gaps hinder her
professional growth, as interpersonal effectiveness is crucial for leadership opportunities, team cohesion,
client satisfaction, and career advancement. Addressing these challenges is essential for Riya to excel
beyond technical competence and become a well-rounded professional.
(b) Suggest at least three practical strategies Riya can use to improve communication and active listening
within her team - Riya can improve communication and active listening using several strategies. First, she
should practice structured participation in meetings, preparing points in advance and gradually
contributing ideas to build confidence. Second, she should adopt active listening techniques, such as
maintaining eye contact, nodding, summarizing key points, and asking clarifying questions, which
demonstrates engagement and reduces misunderstandings. Third, Riya can seek feedback from colleagues
after discussions to understand how her communication is perceived and identify areas for improvement.
Additionally, using collaborative tools for idea-sharing can ensure her input is visible even if she hesitates
to speak in large meetings. Regular reflection on interactions, journaling communication challenges, and
observing effective communicators within her organization can further enhance her skills. Implementing
these strategies consistently will help Riya express herself confidently, understand team dynamics better,
and strengthen professional relationships.
(c) How can empathy and cultural sensitivity help Riya handle client interactions more effectively? -
Empathy allows Riya to understand clients’ perspectives, emotions, and expectations, enabling her to
respond appropriately and build trust. By actively listening and validating clients’ concerns, she can foster
a positive rapport and ensure solutions align with their needs. Cultural sensitivity further enhances
interactions, especially in a diverse client base. Being aware of cultural norms, communication styles, and
values helps Riya avoid misunderstandings, demonstrate respect, and adapt her approach accordingly. For
example, understanding a client’s preference for formal communication or decision-making processes can
prevent friction and miscommunication. By combining empathy with cultural awareness, Riya can
anticipate client concerns, address issues proactively, and strengthen long-term relationships, enhancing
client satisfaction and project outcomes. These skills not only improve professional credibility but also
contribute to her growth as a versatile, globally competent professional.
********** 6 **********
--- page 7 ---
(d) Imagine you are Riya’s mentor. Design a short action plan to enhance her teamwork, assertiveness,
and conflict-resolution skills. As Riya’s mentor, I would create a three-step action plan. First, to improve
teamwork, I would encourage her to actively participate in small group discussions, volunteer for
collaborative tasks, and acknowledge colleagues’ contributions to build mutual trust. Second, to enhance
assertiveness, she should practice expressing opinions clearly in low-stakes settings, gradually increasing
participation in larger meetings, and use “I” statements to communicate ideas confidently without
appearing aggressive. Third, for conflict resolution, I would teach her to listen empathetically, identify
the root cause of disagreements, and facilitate compromise by proposing solutions that respect all
perspectives. Additional steps include role-playing scenarios, seeking constructive feedback, and
reflecting on experiences to identify improvements. Regular check-ins would track progress, ensuring the
plan translates into consistent behavior changes. Implementing this action plan will strengthen Riya’s
interpersonal effectiveness, enabling her to collaborate, lead, and handle workplace challenges gracefully.
6.   Discuss the principles of effective leadership in promoting team collaboration, motivation, and engagement.
(a) Create a comprehensive plan showing how a leader can apply these principles to resolve conflicts, address
disengagement, and improve coordination within teams.
Synoptic Answer:
(a) Principles of Effective Leadership in promoting team collaboration, motivation, and engagement
revolve around setting clear goals, fostering open communication, building trust, and nurturing a positive
environment. These principles help in resolving conflicts, addressing disengagement, and improving
coordination within teams. Core principles include:
Visibility and Availability: Leaders being accessible encourages open dialogue and support.
Effective Communication: Clarity and transparency ensure everyone understands their roles and the team's
objectives.
Trust and Respect: Building trust through consistency and integrity promotes a safe environment for
sharing ideas.
Shared Vision and Goals: Aligning team efforts with common objectives increases motivation.
Empathy and Inclusivity: Recognizing individual differences promotes engagement.
Recognition and Feedback: Regular acknowledgment and constructive feedback boost morale.
Encouraging Innovation: Creating space for creativity energizes teams.
(b) Suggest actionable strategies to develop a positive, productive, and sustainable work environment.
Synoptic Answer:
(b) Comprehensive Plan for Leadership Application:
1. Establish Clear, SMART Goals: Set specific, measurable, attainable, relevant, and time-bound
objectives to guide team efforts and provide clarity.
2. Promote Open Communication: Develop channels for transparent information sharing team meetings,
digital platforms and promote a culture where questions and feedback are welcomed.
3. Promote Trust and Psychological Safety: Lead by example with honesty, integrity, and reliability.
Address conflicts proactively by listening actively to all parties involved.
4. Provide Vision and Purpose: Communicate a compelling vision that aligns with team values, inspiring
intrinsic motivation.
5. Build Inclusive and Respectful Culture: Encourage diversity, active listening, and mutual respect,
ensuring all voices are heard.
6. Recognize Contributions Regularly: Celebrate achievements and provide recognition tailored to
individual and team efforts.
********** 7 **********
--- page 8 ---
7. Develop and Empower Team Members: Invest in ongoing training, mentorship, and personal
development initiatives.
8. Encourage Collaboration and Innovation: Facilitate cross-functional teamwork and creative problem-
solving sessions.
9. Make Data-Informed Decisions: Use feedback and performance data to guide adjustments, ensuring
transparency and shared ownership of outcomes.
10. Promote a Positive Work Environment: Promote work-life balance, emotional intelligence, and
resilience-building activities.
Actionable Strategies to Achieve a Sustainable Work Environment:
- Implement Regular Team Check-ins to address issues and reinforce engagement.
-   Create Collaborative Platforms for knowledge sharing.
-   Offer Conflict Resolution Training to managers and team members.
- Recognize and Reward collaborative behaviors and achievements.
- Promote Work-Life Balance through flexible working arrangements.
- Encourage Personal Development initiatives aligned with organizational goals.
-   Maintain Transparent Policies to promote trust.
This integrated approach, rooted in foundational leadership principles, provides a structured pathway for
leaders to cultivate a positive, productive, and sustainable work environment, ultimately driving
organizational success.
********** 8 **********
````

````text
SYNOPTICS
SVKMs NMIMS
MUKESH PATEL SCH.OF TECH.MGMT ENGG-NM-M
Programme: B. Tech (AI)/B. Tech (AI & DS)/B. Tech (AI & ML)/B. Tech (CIVIL)/B. Tech
(COMP)/B. Tech (CS)/B. Tech (CSE - CS)/B. Tech (DS)/B. Tech (EXTC)/B. Tech Intg. (CE)/B.
Tech Intg. (DS)/B. Tech Intg. (IT)/B. Tech Intg. (Mech)/B. Tech (IT)/B. Tech (MECH)/B. Tech
(MXTC)/MBA Tech (AI)/MBA Tech (Comp)/MBA Tech (DS)/MBA Tech (IT)
Year: III / IV / V/Semester IX/Semester V/Semester VII(Exam Year: 2025-2026)
Subject: Leading through Life Skills
Date: 11 Dec 2025   Time: 02:00 pm to 05:00 pm (03:00 Hrs.)
Max. Marks: 100
FINAL EXAMINATION (Acad. Year:2025-2026)
Instructions:
1. This question paper contains 8 pages.
2. 1. Candidates should read carefully the instructions.
3. 2. Answer to each new question to be started on a fresh page.
4. 3. Figures in brackets against each question indicate full marks.
5. 4. Question No.1 is compulsory. Answer any four questions from the rest questionnaire.
1. Short notes/definitions/concepts (All questions compulsory) 20 Marks
I.   Define emotional agility and differentiate it from emotional intelligence with one example.
Synoptic Answer: Emotional agility = ability to navigate emotions adaptively; EI = recognition +
regulation; example: acknowledging fear before a presentation and acting confidently.
II.   Many people struggle with procrastination. Discuss the causes of procrastination and suggest time
management strategies to overcome it.
Synoptic Answer:
Procrastination is a common challenge where individuals delay important tasks despite knowing the
negative consequences. The causes of procrastination vary, but often include lack of motivation, fear of
failure, perfectionism, poor self-discipline, and distraction from activities like social media. Sometimes
tasks appear overwhelming, causing individuals to avoid them until the last moment. Low energy levels,
ineffective planning, and absence of clear goals can also make people postpone work.
To overcome procrastination, effective time management strategies are essential. Breaking large tasks
into smaller, manageable steps helps reduce the feeling of being overwhelmed. Prioritization tools, such
as the Eisenhower Matrix, can assist in distinguishing urgent and important tasks from less significant
ones. Creating a structured daily schedule with set deadlines encourages consistency. Using techniques
and methods, where work is done in focused intervals with short breaks, improves productivity.
Minimizing distractions, setting realistic goals, and rewarding progress further strengthen self-discipline.
By practicing these strategies, individuals can gradually replace procrastination with purposeful and
timely action.
III.   Explain the how Forced Connections can help in creative problem solving?
Synoptic Answer:
Forced Connections is a creative thinking technique that encourages people to connect unrelated ideas,
objects, or concepts to generate new insights or innovative solutions. It works on the principle that
********** 1 **********
--- page 2 ---
creativity often emerges from unexpected associations, when two seemingly unrelated things are
combined, they can produce a breakthrough idea. In short, it forces the mind to move beyond habitual
patterns of thinking.
It breaks mental fixedness, the tendency to think in predictable, habitual ways.
It stimulates cross-domain thinking, where solutions from one field inspire breakthroughs in another.
It expands associative networks in the brain, unlocking lateral thinking pathways.
It promotes collaborative ideation, since team members interpret connections differently.
IV. “Self-awareness is the foundation of ethical leadership.” Critically evaluate this statement by
integrating insights from Johari Window, Carl Rogers’ Self-Concept, and examples from Indian epics or
corporate leadership.
Synoptic Answer:
Discussed: Self-concept (real, ideal, perceived self); Johari Window’s open, blind, hidden, unknown
areas;
How feedback and self-disclosure foster authentic leadership; relate to Arjuna’s self-realization guided
by Krishna or Satya Nadella’s self-reflective leadership; Conclude on how self-awareness fosters trust,
ethics, and growth.
2. Case-based/scenario analysis & Essay/integrative reflection-application(Attempt Any 4),80
Marks
1.
(a) Case: During a campus project, conflicts arise due to unequal contributions. As the leader, how will
you apply dharma-based reasoning to restore harmony?
Synoptic Answer:
Define dharma (duty-based ethics); apply fairness, accountability, compassion; cite examples from
Ramayana (Rama’s adherence to duty); evaluate outcomes: justice, inclusion, and mutual respect.
(b) Explain how practicing adaptability and inner agility enables engineers to lead innovation in volatile
technological environments.
Synoptic Answer:
Define adaptability and agility; link to self-management; example; Airbnb pivot during COVID-19;
contrast rigidity vs growth mindset; outcome, innovation resilience.
2.
(a) Imagine you are a manager handling multiple projects at once. Some tasks are urgent, while others are
important but less time-sensitive. How will you use prioritization tools (like the Eisenhower Matrix) to
decide where to focus first?
Synoptic Answer:
As a manager handling multiple projects, prioritization is crucial to maintain efficiency and ensure critical
tasks are completed on time. I would use the Eisenhower Matrix to categorize tasks into four quadrants:
Urgent and Important (Do First): Tasks such as meeting client deadlines or resolving a sudden project
issue fall here. These require immediate attention, so I would allocate time and resources to handle them
first.
Important but Not Urgent (Schedule): Activities like strategic planning, skill development of team
members, or building client relationships are less time-sensitive but vital for long-term success. I would
schedule these into my calendar to ensure they are not neglected.
********** 2 **********
--- page 3 ---
Urgent but Not Important (Delegate): Tasks like routine reporting or administrative approvals, though
urgent, may not require my direct involvement. I would delegate them to capable team members, ensuring
they are done without consuming my focus.
Neither Urgent nor Important (Eliminate): Activities such as unnecessary meetings or low-value emails
would be minimized or eliminated to free up time.
By consistently applying this matrix, I can focus on high-impact activities, prevent burnout, and balance
short-term deliverables with long-term organizational goals.
(b) You are dealing with family expectations, career goals, and personal health issues simultaneously.
The stress is affecting your mood and relationships. How would you create a balanced lifestyle plan to
reduce stress, including time for rest, hobbies, and self-care?
Synoptic Answer:
Managing family expectations, career goals, and personal health issues simultaneously requires a balanced
lifestyle plan that addresses both responsibilities and well-being. First, I would start with self-awareness,
identifying stress triggers such as overcommitment, lack of rest, or unrealistic expectations. By clarifying
priorities, I can set realistic goals for career progress while maintaining family harmony and focusing on
health recovery.
I would design a structured daily routine that allocates specific time blocks for work, family interactions,
and self-care. Professional tasks would be scheduled during peak productivity hours, while family time
could be reserved for evenings or weekends to strengthen relationships. To protect my health, I would
include regular exercise, a balanced diet, and sufficient sleep in the plan, since physical well-being
enhances emotional resilience.
Equally important, I would dedicate time to rest and hobbies, such as reading, music, or outdoor activities,
to recharge mentally and emotionally. Practicing mindfulness or meditation would also help regulate
mood and reduce stress.
Finally, I would learn to set boundaries and delegate when necessary, ensuring I don’t shoulder every
responsibility alone. By balancing obligations with relaxation and personal growth, I can maintain better
relationships, improve my health, and pursue career goals effectively.
3.

Synoptic Answer:
(a) Identify the key interpersonal skill challenges faced by Riya and explain how they impact her
professional growth. - Riya’s primary interpersonal challenges include hesitation to express ideas, limited
active listening, low assertiveness, and insufficient empathy. Hesitation in sharing ideas during meetings
prevents her from contributing valuable insights, reducing her visibility and influence in team discussions.
Limited active listening and understanding of colleagues’ and clients’ perspectives lead to
miscommunication, project delays, and diminished collaboration. Low assertiveness means she struggles
to voice concerns or negotiate effectively, impacting her ability to lead or manage responsibilities
confidently. Insufficient empathy affects her client interactions and teamwork, as she may fail to
recognize others’ needs or emotions, lowering trust and rapport. Collectively, these gaps hinder her
professional growth, as interpersonal effectiveness is crucial for leadership opportunities, team cohesion,
client satisfaction, and career advancement. Addressing these challenges is essential for Riya to excel
beyond technical competence and become a well-rounded professional.
(b) Suggest at least three practical strategies Riya can use to improve communication and active listening
within her team - Riya can improve communication and active listening using several strategies. First, she
should practice structured participation in meetings, preparing points in advance and gradually
contributing ideas to build confidence. Second, she should adopt active listening techniques, such as
maintaining eye contact, nodding, summarizing key points, and asking clarifying questions, which
demonstrates engagement and reduces misunderstandings. Third, Riya can seek feedback from colleagues
after discussions to understand how her communication is perceived and identify areas for improvement.
Additionally, using collaborative tools for idea-sharing can ensure her input is visible even if she hesitates
to speak in large meetings. Regular reflection on interactions, journaling communication challenges, and
observing effective communicators within her organization can further enhance her skills. Implementing
these strategies consistently will help Riya express herself confidently, understand team dynamics better,
and strengthen professional relationships.
(c) How can empathy and cultural sensitivity help Riya handle client interactions more effectively? -
Empathy allows Riya to understand clients’ perspectives, emotions, and expectations, enabling her to
respond appropriately and build trust. By actively listening and validating clients’ concerns, she can foster
a positive rapport and ensure solutions align with their needs. Cultural sensitivity further enhances
interactions, especially in a diverse client base. Being aware of cultural norms, communication styles, and
values helps Riya avoid misunderstandings, demonstrate respect, and adapt her approach accordingly. For
example, understanding a client’s preference for formal communication or decision-making processes can
prevent friction and miscommunication. By combining empathy with cultural awareness, Riya can
anticipate client concerns, address issues proactively, and strengthen long-term relationships, enhancing
client satisfaction and project outcomes. These skills not only improve professional credibility but also
contribute to her growth as a versatile, globally competent professional.
********** 6 **********
--- page 7 ---
(d) Imagine you are Riya’s mentor. Design a short action plan to enhance her teamwork, assertiveness,
and conflict-resolution skills. As Riya’s mentor, I would create a three-step action plan. First, to improve
teamwork, I would encourage her to actively participate in small group discussions, volunteer for
collaborative tasks, and acknowledge colleagues’ contributions to build mutual trust. Second, to enhance
assertiveness, she should practice expressing opinions clearly in low-stakes settings, gradually increasing
participation in larger meetings, and use “I” statements to communicate ideas confidently without
appearing aggressive. Third, for conflict resolution, I would teach her to listen empathetically, identify
the root cause of disagreements, and facilitate compromise by proposing solutions that respect all
perspectives. Additional steps include role-playing scenarios, seeking constructive feedback, and
reflecting on experiences to identify improvements. Regular check-ins would track progress, ensuring the
plan translates into consistent behavior changes. Implementing this action plan will strengthen Riya’s
interpersonal effectiveness, enabling her to collaborate, lead, and handle workplace challenges gracefully.
6.   Discuss the principles of effective leadership in promoting team collaboration, motivation, and engagement.
(a) Create a comprehensive plan showing how a leader can apply these principles to resolve conflicts, address
disengagement, and improve coordination within teams.
Synoptic Answer:
(a) Principles of Effective Leadership in promoting team collaboration, motivation, and engagement
revolve around setting clear goals, fostering open communication, building trust, and nurturing a positive
environment. These principles help in resolving conflicts, addressing disengagement, and improving
coordination within teams. Core principles include:
Visibility and Availability: Leaders being accessible encourages open dialogue and support.
Effective Communication: Clarity and transparency ensure everyone understands their roles and the team's
objectives.
Trust and Respect: Building trust through consistency and integrity promotes a safe environment for
sharing ideas.
Shared Vision and Goals: Aligning team efforts with common objectives increases motivation.
Empathy and Inclusivity: Recognizing individual differences promotes engagement.
Recognition and Feedback: Regular acknowledgment and constructive feedback boost morale.
Encouraging Innovation: Creating space for creativity energizes teams.
(b) Suggest actionable strategies to develop a positive, productive, and sustainable work environment.
Synoptic Answer:
(b) Comprehensive Plan for Leadership Application:
1. Establish Clear, SMART Goals: Set specific, measurable, attainable, relevant, and time-bound
objectives to guide team efforts and provide clarity.
2. Promote Open Communication: Develop channels for transparent information sharing team meetings,
digital platforms and promote a culture where questions and feedback are welcomed.
3. Promote Trust and Psychological Safety: Lead by example with honesty, integrity, and reliability.
Address conflicts proactively by listening actively to all parties involved.
4. Provide Vision and Purpose: Communicate a compelling vision that aligns with team values, inspiring
intrinsic motivation.
5. Build Inclusive and Respectful Culture: Encourage diversity, active listening, and mutual respect,
ensuring all voices are heard.
6. Recognize Contributions Regularly: Celebrate achievements and provide recognition tailored to
individual and team efforts.
********** 7 **********
--- page 8 ---
7. Develop and Empower Team Members: Invest in ongoing training, mentorship, and personal
development initiatives.
8. Encourage Collaboration and Innovation: Facilitate cross-functional teamwork and creative problem-
solving sessions.
9. Make Data-Informed Decisions: Use feedback and performance data to guide adjustments, ensuring
transparency and shared ownership of outcomes.
10. Promote a Positive Work Environment: Promote work-life balance, emotional intelligence, and
resilience-building activities.
Actionable Strategies to Achieve a Sustainable Work Environment:
- Implement Regular Team Check-ins to address issues and reinforce engagement.
-   Create Collaborative Platforms for knowledge sharing.
-   Offer Conflict Resolution Training to managers and team members.
- Recognize and Reward collaborative behaviors and achievements.
- Promote Work-Life Balance through flexible working arrangements.
- Encourage Personal Development initiatives aligned with organizational goals.
-   Maintain Transparent Policies to promote trust.
This integrated approach, rooted in foundational leadership principles, provides a structured pathway for
leaders to cultivate a positive, productive, and sustainable work environment, ultimately driving
organizational success.
********** 8 **********
````

## 2025-26 · Final-Exam · Sem 7 · Answer key · paper 3fdf37d40afd

- Departments: Data Science

- Text: text (8 pages)

````text
SYNOPTICS
SVKMs NMIMS
MUKESH PATEL SCH.OF TECH.MGMT ENGG-NM-M
Programme: B. Tech (AI)/B. Tech (AI & DS)/B. Tech (AI & ML)/B. Tech (CIVIL)/B. Tech
(COMP)/B. Tech (CS)/B. Tech (CSE - CS)/B. Tech (DS)/B. Tech (EXTC)/B. Tech Intg. (CE)/B.
Tech Intg. (DS)/B. Tech Intg. (IT)/B. Tech Intg. (Mech)/B. Tech (IT)/B. Tech (MECH)/B. Tech
(MXTC)/MBA Tech (AI)/MBA Tech (Comp)/MBA Tech (DS)/MBA Tech (IT)
Year: III / IV / V/Semester IX/Semester V/Semester VII(Exam Year: 2025-2026)
Subject: Leading through Life Skills
Date: 11 Dec 2025   Time: 02:00 pm to 05:00 pm (03:00 Hrs.)
Max. Marks: 100
FINAL EXAMINATION (Acad. Year:2025-2026)
Instructions:
1. This question paper contains 8 pages.
2. 1. Candidates should read carefully the instructions.
3. 2. Answer to each new question to be started on a fresh page.
4. 3. Figures in brackets against each question indicate full marks.
5. 4. Question No.1 is compulsory. Answer any four questions from the rest questionnaire.
1. Short notes/definitions/concepts (All questions compulsory) 20 Marks
I.   Define emotional agility and differentiate it from emotional intelligence with one example.
Synoptic Answer: Emotional agility = ability to navigate emotions adaptively; EI = recognition +
regulation; example: acknowledging fear before a presentation and acting confidently.
II.   Many people struggle with procrastination. Discuss the causes of procrastination and suggest time
management strategies to overcome it.
Synoptic Answer:
Procrastination is a common challenge where individuals delay important tasks despite knowing the
negative consequences. The causes of procrastination vary, but often include lack of motivation, fear of
failure, perfectionism, poor self-discipline, and distraction from activities like social media. Sometimes
tasks appear overwhelming, causing individuals to avoid them until the last moment. Low energy levels,
ineffective planning, and absence of clear goals can also make people postpone work.
To overcome procrastination, effective time management strategies are essential. Breaking large tasks
into smaller, manageable steps helps reduce the feeling of being overwhelmed. Prioritization tools, such
as the Eisenhower Matrix, can assist in distinguishing urgent and important tasks from less significant
ones. Creating a structured daily schedule with set deadlines encourages consistency. Using techniques
and methods, where work is done in focused intervals with short breaks, improves productivity.
Minimizing distractions, setting realistic goals, and rewarding progress further strengthen self-discipline.
By practicing these strategies, individuals can gradually replace procrastination with purposeful and
timely action.
III.   Explain the how Forced Connections can help in creative problem solving?
Synoptic Answer:
Forced Connections is a creative thinking technique that encourages people to connect unrelated ideas,
objects, or concepts to generate new insights or innovative solutions. It works on the principle that
********** 1 **********
--- page 2 ---
creativity often emerges from unexpected associations, when two seemingly unrelated things are
combined, they can produce a breakthrough idea. In short, it forces the mind to move beyond habitual
patterns of thinking.
It breaks mental fixedness, the tendency to think in predictable, habitual ways.
It stimulates cross-domain thinking, where solutions from one field inspire breakthroughs in another.
It expands associative networks in the brain, unlocking lateral thinking pathways.
It promotes collaborative ideation, since team members interpret connections differently.
IV. “Self-awareness is the foundation of ethical leadership.” Critically evaluate this statement by
integrating insights from Johari Window, Carl Rogers’ Self-Concept, and examples from Indian epics or
corporate leadership.
Synoptic Answer:
Discussed: Self-concept (real, ideal, perceived self); Johari Window’s open, blind, hidden, unknown
areas;
How feedback and self-disclosure foster authentic leadership; relate to Arjuna’s self-realization guided
by Krishna or Satya Nadella’s self-reflective leadership; Conclude on how self-awareness fosters trust,
ethics, and growth.
2. Case-based/scenario analysis & Essay/integrative reflection-application(Attempt Any 4),80
Marks
1.
(a) Case: During a campus project, conflicts arise due to unequal contributions. As the leader, how will
you apply dharma-based reasoning to restore harmony?
Synoptic Answer:
Define dharma (duty-based ethics); apply fairness, accountability, compassion; cite examples from
Ramayana (Rama’s adherence to duty); evaluate outcomes: justice, inclusion, and mutual respect.
(b) Explain how practicing adaptability and inner agility enables engineers to lead innovation in volatile
technological environments.
Synoptic Answer:
Define adaptability and agility; link to self-management; example; Airbnb pivot during COVID-19;
contrast rigidity vs growth mindset; outcome, innovation resilience.
2.
(a) Imagine you are a manager handling multiple projects at once. Some tasks are urgent, while others are
important but less time-sensitive. How will you use prioritization tools (like the Eisenhower Matrix) to
decide where to focus first?
Synoptic Answer:
As a manager handling multiple projects, prioritization is crucial to maintain efficiency and ensure critical
tasks are completed on time. I would use the Eisenhower Matrix to categorize tasks into four quadrants:
Urgent and Important (Do First): Tasks such as meeting client deadlines or resolving a sudden project
issue fall here. These require immediate attention, so I would allocate time and resources to handle them
first.
Important but Not Urgent (Schedule): Activities like strategic planning, skill development of team
members, or building client relationships are less time-sensitive but vital for long-term success. I would
schedule these into my calendar to ensure they are not neglected.
********** 2 **********
--- page 3 ---
Urgent but Not Important (Delegate): Tasks like routine reporting or administrative approvals, though
urgent, may not require my direct involvement. I would delegate them to capable team members, ensuring
they are done without consuming my focus.
Neither Urgent nor Important (Eliminate): Activities such as unnecessary meetings or low-value emails
would be minimized or eliminated to free up time.
By consistently applying this matrix, I can focus on high-impact activities, prevent burnout, and balance
short-term deliverables with long-term organizational goals.
(b) You are dealing with family expectations, career goals, and personal health issues simultaneously.
The stress is affecting your mood and relationships. How would you create a balanced lifestyle plan to
reduce stress, including time for rest, hobbies, and self-care?
Synoptic Answer:
Managing family expectations, career goals, and personal health issues simultaneously requires a balanced
lifestyle plan that addresses both responsibilities and well-being. First, I would start with self-awareness,
identifying stress triggers such as overcommitment, lack of rest, or unrealistic expectations. By clarifying
priorities, I can set realistic goals for career progress while maintaining family harmony and focusing on
health recovery.
I would design a structured daily routine that allocates specific time blocks for work, family interactions,
and self-care. Professional tasks would be scheduled during peak productivity hours, while family time
could be reserved for evenings or weekends to strengthen relationships. To protect my health, I would
include regular exercise, a balanced diet, and sufficient sleep in the plan, since physical well-being
enhances emotional resilience.
Equally important, I would dedicate time to rest and hobbies, such as reading, music, or outdoor activities,
to recharge mentally and emotionally. Practicing mindfulness or meditation would also help regulate
mood and reduce stress.
Finally, I would learn to set boundaries and delegate when necessary, ensuring I don’t shoulder every
responsibility alone. By balancing obligations with relaxation and personal growth, I can maintain better
relationships, improve my health, and pursue career goals effectively.
3.
(a) Read the following case and answer the questions mentioned below: Rohan is a senior sales executive
at EcoPrint Solutions, a company that sells sustainable printing systems to corporate offices. EcoPrint’s
flagship product, GreenEdge X5, uses recycled cartridges and consumes 40% less electricity than
conventional printers. The machine costs ₹2.5 lakh per unit, about 25% higher than regular printers. Rohan
has secured a meeting with NovaCorp, a mid-sized financial consultancy that prints thousands of pages
every week. The procurement head, Meera Sharma, is interested in exploring sustainable alternatives but
faces strict cost-cutting directives from management. NovaCorp’s current supplier has offered a lower
price and long-term service contract, making Meera hesitant to switch. Rohan must convince Meera that
GreenEdge X5 is not just an eco-friendly choice but a financially smart one. Meera, on the other hand,
wants solid evidence that the product will reduce long-term expenses and align with NovaCorp’s brand
image of responsible business practices. How can Rohan design and justify a persuasive sales strategy for
GreenEdge X5 by integrating and evaluating the three elements of Aristotle’s persuasion model,
credibility, emotion, and logic, to effectively influence Meera’s decision?
Synoptic Answer:
Rohan’s objective is to design a persuasive sales strategy that convinces Meera Sharma at NovaCorp to
approve a pilot purchase of the GreenEdge X5 printer, despite its higher price. To achieve this, he must
********** 3 **********
--- page 4 ---
combine credibility (Ethos), emotion (Pathos), and logic (Logos) into a coherent, ethical, and compelling
pitch that aligns with NovaCorp’s business and sustainability goals.
1. Establishing Credibility (Ethos)
Rohan should first position himself and EcoPrint Solutions as trustworthy and competent partners.
Professional credibility: Begin by briefly sharing EcoPrint’s track record—years in operation, key
corporate clients, and sustainability awards.
Personal credibility: Emphasize his technical expertise, customer success stories, and commitment to
post-sale service.
Ethical transparency: Acknowledge that the printer costs 25 percent more upfront but justify this honestly
as a long-term investment.
By demonstrating integrity, competence, and reliability, Rohan reduces perceived risk and builds Meera’s
confidence in both the product and the relationship.
2. Appealing to Emotion and Shared Values (Pathos)
To move beyond rational comparison, Rohan should connect the offer to NovaCorp’s identity and
aspirations.
Shared values: Link GreenEdge X5 to NovaCorp’s stated commitment to corporate responsibility and
sustainability reporting.
Vision narrative: Paint a picture of NovaCorp as a leader adopting green technologies that clients and
regulators will admire.
Empathy and relevance: Recognize Meera’s cost-cutting pressures and show understanding of her
accountability, positioning EcoPrint as a supportive partner, not a vendor. Visuals, images of reduced
carbon footprint, testimonials from employees proud of their firm’s green initiatives, can reinforce
emotional   engagement.
A well-crafted emotional connection transforms the proposal from a transaction into a partnership for a
cleaner, modern workplace.
3. Presenting Logical Evidence (Logos)
Rohan must provide concrete, data-driven proof that GreenEdge X5 is economically and operationally
superior.
Quantitative comparisons: Demonstrate 40 percent lower power consumption and 30 percent longer
cartridge life, translating these figures into annual cost savings.
ROI projection: Prepare a simple five-year cost-of-ownership chart showing payback within 18 months
due to energy and consumable savings.
Case study evidence: Present data from another client of similar scale where operational costs dropped
and employee satisfaction rose.
Logical clarity reinforces that sustainability and profitability can coexist, satisfying Meera’s need for
measurable justification.
(b) Case Study: A mid-level manager is tasked with improving team performance and workplace culture,
but some team members are skeptical about adopting new leadership practices.
Evaluate how Spiritual Leadership can help leaders inspire ethical behavior, motivate teams, and create a long-
term positive organizational culture.
Synoptic Answer:
Spiritual Leadership can significantly help a mid-level manager improve team performance and workplace
culture by inspiring ethical behavior, motivating teams intrinsically, and creating a long-term positive
organizational culture. It does so by fostering a sense of meaning, purpose, and connection to a shared
vision within the team, while emphasizing core spiritual values such as altruistic love, integrity, honesty,
and care for others.
********** 4 **********
--- page 5 ---
Inspiring Ethical Behavior -Spiritual leadership emphasizes ethical standards like transparency, honesty,
integrity, and commitment as foundational elements. This encourages team members to align their
personal and professional values with the organization’s ethical norms, reducing skepticism about new
leadership practices and promoting trust among team members.
Motivating Teams - Intrinsic motivation leads to better task performance, knowledge sharing, and
creativity.
Creating Long-term Positive Culture - Spiritual leadership promotes a workplace culture grounded in
shared vision, hope, faith, and altruistic love. This culture supports employees’ well-being, reduces stress
and emotional fatigue, and nurtures psychological safety. Over time, this leads to higher job satisfaction,
lower turnover, and sustained commitment to the organization’s mission. Employees feel valued,
connected, and driven by purpose, which enhances loyalty and meaningful contributions, ultimately
producing a resilient, positive organizational culture.
Practical Steps for the Manager - Communicate a clear, inspiring vision that connects team members to
a larger purpose beyond daily tasks. Model and reinforce ethical behaviors consistently. Show authentic
care for team members’ growth and well-being. Encourage open communication and psychological safety
to include all voices. Promote a supportive community that shares spiritual values aligned with
organizational goals.
Thus, spiritual leadership offers a powerful framework for the manager to transform team dynamics
ethically and motivationally while building a cohesive long-term culture.
4.   Ritika, a project manager, must decide whether to launch a new product prototype that passed
testing but still faces market uncertainty. Her team is divided, some see opportunity, others warn of risk.
How can Ritika apply all six thinking hats, white, red, black, yellow, green, and blue; to evaluate facts,
emotions, risks, opportunities, creativity, and process control for making a well-balanced decision?
Synoptic Answer:
Ritika can use all six thinking hats to make a balanced and well-informed decision.
Under the White Hat, she should focus on facts and data, market trends, testing results, and competitor
insights.
The Red Hat lets her and her team express gut feelings or anxieties about the launch honestly.
Using the Black Hat, she evaluates possible risks such as poor market response, high costs, or supply
delays.
The Yellow Hat helps her identify potential benefits like brand visibility, innovation leadership, and
customer excitement.
With the Green Hat, she can brainstorm creative strategies, such as limited trials or collaborations, to
reduce risk while testing the market.
Finally, the Blue Hat guides the overall process, organizing discussions, summarizing viewpoints, and
helping the team reach a clear, structured decision.
5.   Riya is a marketing executive at a growing IT firm. Although she is technically competent and meets
her targets, she faces challenges in building strong relationships with her colleagues and clients. During
team meetings, she often hesitates to share her ideas, fearing criticism, while some team members
dominate discussions. Recently, a client feedback session revealed that Riya struggled to understand the

while Riya excels in technical tasks, her limited interpersonal skills, such as communication, active
listening, empathy, and teamwork, affect collaboration and client satisfaction. Riya wants to improve her
interpersonal effectiveness to strengthen professional relationships, contribute more actively in meetings,
********** 5 **********
--- page 6 ---
and handle conflicts or misunderstandings gracefully. She seeks guidance on practical strategies to
enhance her skills in real workplace situations.
````
