````text
Programme: B. Tech (AI)/B. Tech (AI & DS)/B. Tech (AI & ML)/B. Tech (CIVIL)/B. Tech
(COMP)/B. Tech (CS)/B. Tech (CSE - CS)/B. Tech (DS)/B. Tech (EXTC)/B. Tech Intg. (CE)/B.
Tech Intg. (DS)/B. Tech Intg. (IT)/B. Tech Intg. (Mech)/B. Tech (IT)/B. Tech (MECH)/B. Tech
(MXTC)/MBA Tech (AI)/MBA Tech (Comp)/MBA Tech (DS)/MBA Tech (IT)
Year: III / IV / V/Semester IX/Semester V/Semester VII(Exam Year: 2025-2026)
Subject: Leading through Life Skills
Date: 11 Dec 2025   Time: 02:00 pm to 05:00 pm (03:00 Hrs.)
Max. Marks: 100
FINAL EXAMINATION (Acad. Year:2025-2026)
Instructions:
1. This question paper contains 8 pages.
2. 1. Candidates should read carefully the instructions.
3. 2. Answer to each new question to be started on a fresh page.
4. 3. Figures in brackets against each question indicate full marks.
5. 4. Question No.1 is compulsory. Answer any four questions from the rest questionnaire.
1. Short notes/definitions/concepts (All questions compulsory) 20 Marks
I.   Define emotional agility and differentiate it from emotional intelligence with one example.
Synoptic Answer: Emotional agility = ability to navigate emotions adaptively; EI = recognition +
regulation; example: acknowledging fear before a presentation and acting confidently.
II.   Many people struggle with procrastination. Discuss the causes of procrastination and suggest time
management strategies to overcome it.
Synoptic Answer:
Procrastination is a common challenge where individuals delay important tasks despite knowing the
negative consequences. The causes of procrastination vary, but often include lack of motivation, fear of
failure, perfectionism, poor self-discipline, and distraction from activities like social media. Sometimes
tasks appear overwhelming, causing individuals to avoid them until the last moment. Low energy levels,
ineffective planning, and absence of clear goals can also make people postpone work.
To overcome procrastination, effective time management strategies are essential. Breaking large tasks
into smaller, manageable steps helps reduce the feeling of being overwhelmed. Prioritization tools, such
as the Eisenhower Matrix, can assist in distinguishing urgent and important tasks from less significant
ones. Creating a structured daily schedule with set deadlines encourages consistency. Using techniques
and methods, where work is done in focused intervals with short breaks, improves productivity.
Minimizing distractions, setting realistic goals, and rewarding progress further strengthen self-discipline.
By practicing these strategies, individuals can gradually replace procrastination with purposeful and
timely action.
III.   Explain the how Forced Connections can help in creative problem solving?
Synoptic Answer:
Forced Connections is a creative thinking technique that encourages people to connect unrelated ideas,
objects, or concepts to generate new insights or innovative solutions. It works on the principle that
********** 1 **********
--- page 2 ---
creativity often emerges from unexpected associations, when two seemingly unrelated things are
combined, they can produce a breakthrough idea. In short, it forces the mind to move beyond habitual
patterns of thinking.
It breaks mental fixedness, the tendency to think in predictable, habitual ways.
It stimulates cross-domain thinking, where solutions from one field inspire breakthroughs in another.
It expands associative networks in the brain, unlocking lateral thinking pathways.
It promotes collaborative ideation, since team members interpret connections differently.
IV. “Self-awareness is the foundation of ethical leadership.” Critically evaluate this statement by
integrating insights from Johari Window, Carl Rogers’ Self-Concept, and examples from Indian epics or
corporate leadership.
Synoptic Answer:
Discussed: Self-concept (real, ideal, perceived self); Johari Window’s open, blind, hidden, unknown
areas;
How feedback and self-disclosure foster authentic leadership; relate to Arjuna’s self-realization guided
by Krishna or Satya Nadella’s self-reflective leadership; Conclude on how self-awareness fosters trust,
ethics, and growth.
2. Case-based/scenario analysis & Essay/integrative reflection-application(Attempt Any 4),80
Marks
1.
(a) Case: During a campus project, conflicts arise due to unequal contributions. As the leader, how will
you apply dharma-based reasoning to restore harmony?
Synoptic Answer:
Define dharma (duty-based ethics); apply fairness, accountability, compassion; cite examples from
Ramayana (Rama’s adherence to duty); evaluate outcomes: justice, inclusion, and mutual respect.
(b) Explain how practicing adaptability and inner agility enables engineers to lead innovation in volatile
technological environments.
Synoptic Answer:
Define adaptability and agility; link to self-management; example; Airbnb pivot during COVID-19;
contrast rigidity vs growth mindset; outcome, innovation resilience.
2.
(a) Imagine you are a manager handling multiple projects at once. Some tasks are urgent, while others are
important but less time-sensitive. How will you use prioritization tools (like the Eisenhower Matrix) to
decide where to focus first?
Synoptic Answer:
As a manager handling multiple projects, prioritization is crucial to maintain efficiency and ensure critical
tasks are completed on time. I would use the Eisenhower Matrix to categorize tasks into four quadrants:
Urgent and Important (Do First): Tasks such as meeting client deadlines or resolving a sudden project
issue fall here. These require immediate attention, so I would allocate time and resources to handle them
first.
Important but Not Urgent (Schedule): Activities like strategic planning, skill development of team
members, or building client relationships are less time-sensitive but vital for long-term success. I would
schedule these into my calendar to ensure they are not neglected.
********** 2 **********
--- page 3 ---
Urgent but Not Important (Delegate): Tasks like routine reporting or administrative approvals, though
urgent, may not require my direct involvement. I would delegate them to capable team members, ensuring
they are done without consuming my focus.
Neither Urgent nor Important (Eliminate): Activities such as unnecessary meetings or low-value emails
would be minimized or eliminated to free up time.
By consistently applying this matrix, I can focus on high-impact activities, prevent burnout, and balance
short-term deliverables with long-term organizational goals.
(b) You are dealing with family expectations, career goals, and personal health issues simultaneously.
The stress is affecting your mood and relationships. How would you create a balanced lifestyle plan to
reduce stress, including time for rest, hobbies, and self-care?
Synoptic Answer:
Managing family expectations, career goals, and personal health issues simultaneously requires a balanced
lifestyle plan that addresses both responsibilities and well-being. First, I would start with self-awareness,
identifying stress triggers such as overcommitment, lack of rest, or unrealistic expectations. By clarifying
priorities, I can set realistic goals for career progress while maintaining family harmony and focusing on
health recovery.
I would design a structured daily routine that allocates specific time blocks for work, family interactions,
and self-care. Professional tasks would be scheduled during peak productivity hours, while family time
could be reserved for evenings or weekends to strengthen relationships. To protect my health, I would
include regular exercise, a balanced diet, and sufficient sleep in the plan, since physical well-being
enhances emotional resilience.
Equally important, I would dedicate time to rest and hobbies, such as reading, music, or outdoor activities,
to recharge mentally and emotionally. Practicing mindfulness or meditation would also help regulate
mood and reduce stress.
Finally, I would learn to set boundaries and delegate when necessary, ensuring I don’t shoulder every
responsibility alone. By balancing obligations with relaxation and personal growth, I can maintain better
relationships, improve my health, and pursue career goals effectively.
3.
(a) Read the following case and answer the questions mentioned below: Rohan is a senior sales executive
at EcoPrint Solutions, a company that sells sustainable printing systems to corporate offices. EcoPrint’s
flagship product, GreenEdge X5, uses recycled cartridges and consumes 40% less electricity than
conventional printers. The machine costs ₹2.5 lakh per unit, about 25% higher than regular printers. Rohan
has secured a meeting with NovaCorp, a mid-sized financial consultancy that prints thousands of pages
every week. The procurement head, Meera Sharma, is interested in exploring sustainable alternatives but
faces strict cost-cutting directives from management. NovaCorp’s current supplier has offered a lower
price and long-term service contract, making Meera hesitant to switch. Rohan must convince Meera that
GreenEdge X5 is not just an eco-friendly choice but a financially smart one. Meera, on the other hand,
wants solid evidence that the product will reduce long-term expenses and align with NovaCorp’s brand
image of responsible business practices. How can Rohan design and justify a persuasive sales strategy for
GreenEdge X5 by integrating and evaluating the three elements of Aristotle’s persuasion model,
credibility, emotion, and logic, to effectively influence Meera’s decision?
Synoptic Answer:
Rohan’s objective is to design a persuasive sales strategy that convinces Meera Sharma at NovaCorp to
approve a pilot purchase of the GreenEdge X5 printer, despite its higher price. To achieve this, he must
********** 3 **********
--- page 4 ---
combine credibility (Ethos), emotion (Pathos), and logic (Logos) into a coherent, ethical, and compelling
pitch that aligns with NovaCorp’s business and sustainability goals.
1. Establishing Credibility (Ethos)
Rohan should first position himself and EcoPrint Solutions as trustworthy and competent partners.
Professional credibility: Begin by briefly sharing EcoPrint’s track record—years in operation, key
corporate clients, and sustainability awards.
Personal credibility: Emphasize his technical expertise, customer success stories, and commitment to
post-sale service.
Ethical transparency: Acknowledge that the printer costs 25 percent more upfront but justify this honestly
as a long-term investment.
By demonstrating integrity, competence, and reliability, Rohan reduces perceived risk and builds Meera’s
confidence in both the product and the relationship.
2. Appealing to Emotion and Shared Values (Pathos)
To move beyond rational comparison, Rohan should connect the offer to NovaCorp’s identity and
aspirations.
Shared values: Link GreenEdge X5 to NovaCorp’s stated commitment to corporate responsibility and
sustainability reporting.
Vision narrative: Paint a picture of NovaCorp as a leader adopting green technologies that clients and
regulators will admire.
Empathy and relevance: Recognize Meera’s cost-cutting pressures and show understanding of her
accountability, positioning EcoPrint as a supportive partner, not a vendor. Visuals, images of reduced
carbon footprint, testimonials from employees proud of their firm’s green initiatives, can reinforce
emotional   engagement.
A well-crafted emotional connection transforms the proposal from a transaction into a partnership for a
cleaner, modern workplace.
3. Presenting Logical Evidence (Logos)
Rohan must provide concrete, data-driven proof that GreenEdge X5 is economically and operationally
superior.
Quantitative comparisons: Demonstrate 40 percent lower power consumption and 30 percent longer
cartridge life, translating these figures into annual cost savings.
ROI projection: Prepare a simple five-year cost-of-ownership chart showing payback within 18 months
due to energy and consumable savings.
Case study evidence: Present data from another client of similar scale where operational costs dropped
and employee satisfaction rose.
Logical clarity reinforces that sustainability and profitability can coexist, satisfying Meera’s need for
measurable justification.
(b) Case Study: A mid-level manager is tasked with improving team performance and workplace culture,
but some team members are skeptical about adopting new leadership practices.
Evaluate how Spiritual Leadership can help leaders inspire ethical behavior, motivate teams, and create a long-
term positive organizational culture.
Synoptic Answer:
Spiritual Leadership can significantly help a mid-level manager improve team performance and workplace
culture by inspiring ethical behavior, motivating teams intrinsically, and creating a long-term positive
organizational culture. It does so by fostering a sense of meaning, purpose, and connection to a shared
vision within the team, while emphasizing core spiritual values such as altruistic love, integrity, honesty,
and care for others.
********** 4 **********
--- page 5 ---
Inspiring Ethical Behavior -Spiritual leadership emphasizes ethical standards like transparency, honesty,
integrity, and commitment as foundational elements. This encourages team members to align their
personal and professional values with the organization’s ethical norms, reducing skepticism about new
leadership practices and promoting trust among team members.
Motivating Teams - Intrinsic motivation leads to better task performance, knowledge sharing, and
creativity.
Creating Long-term Positive Culture - Spiritual leadership promotes a workplace culture grounded in
shared vision, hope, faith, and altruistic love. This culture supports employees’ well-being, reduces stress
and emotional fatigue, and nurtures psychological safety. Over time, this leads to higher job satisfaction,
lower turnover, and sustained commitment to the organization’s mission. Employees feel valued,
connected, and driven by purpose, which enhances loyalty and meaningful contributions, ultimately
producing a resilient, positive organizational culture.
Practical Steps for the Manager - Communicate a clear, inspiring vision that connects team members to
a larger purpose beyond daily tasks. Model and reinforce ethical behaviors consistently. Show authentic
care for team members’ growth and well-being. Encourage open communication and psychological safety
to include all voices. Promote a supportive community that shares spiritual values aligned with
organizational goals.
Thus, spiritual leadership offers a powerful framework for the manager to transform team dynamics
ethically and motivationally while building a cohesive long-term culture.
4.   Ritika, a project manager, must decide whether to launch a new product prototype that passed
testing but still faces market uncertainty. Her team is divided, some see opportunity, others warn of risk.
How can Ritika apply all six thinking hats, white, red, black, yellow, green, and blue; to evaluate facts,
emotions, risks, opportunities, creativity, and process control for making a well-balanced decision?
Synoptic Answer:
Ritika can use all six thinking hats to make a balanced and well-informed decision.
Under the White Hat, she should focus on facts and data, market trends, testing results, and competitor
insights.
The Red Hat lets her and her team express gut feelings or anxieties about the launch honestly.
Using the Black Hat, she evaluates possible risks such as poor market response, high costs, or supply
delays.
The Yellow Hat helps her identify potential benefits like brand visibility, innovation leadership, and
customer excitement.
With the Green Hat, she can brainstorm creative strategies, such as limited trials or collaborations, to
reduce risk while testing the market.
Finally, the Blue Hat guides the overall process, organizing discussions, summarizing viewpoints, and
helping the team reach a clear, structured decision.
5.   Riya is a marketing executive at a growing IT firm. Although she is technically competent and meets
her targets, she faces challenges in building strong relationships with her colleagues and clients. During
team meetings, she often hesitates to share her ideas, fearing criticism, while some team members
dominate discussions. Recently, a client feedback session revealed that Riya struggled to understand the
client’s perspective, leading to miscommunication and a minor project delay. Her manager notices that
while Riya excels in technical tasks, her limited interpersonal skills, such as communication, active
listening, empathy, and teamwork, affect collaboration and client satisfaction. Riya wants to improve her
interpersonal effectiveness to strengthen professional relationships, contribute more actively in meetings,
********** 5 **********
--- page 6 ---
and handle conflicts or misunderstandings gracefully. She seeks guidance on practical strategies to
enhance her skills in real workplace situations.
(a) Identify the key interpersonal skill challenges faced by Riya and explain how they impact her
professional growth.
(b) Suggest at least three practical strategies Riya can use to improve communication and active listening
within her team.
(c) How can empathy and cultural sensitivity help Riya handle client interactions more effectively?
(d) Imagine you are Riya’s mentor. Design a short action plan to enhance her teamwork, assertiveness,
and conflict-resolution skills.
Synoptic Answer:
(a) Identify the key interpersonal skill challenges faced by Riya and explain how they impact her
professional growth. - Riya’s primary interpersonal challenges include hesitation to express ideas, limited
active listening, low assertiveness, and insufficient empathy. Hesitation in sharing ideas during meetings
prevents her from contributing valuable insights, reducing her visibility and influence in team discussions.
Limited active listening and understanding of colleagues’ and clients’ perspectives lead to
miscommunication, project delays, and diminished collaboration. Low assertiveness means she struggles
to voice concerns or negotiate effectively, impacting her ability to lead or manage responsibilities
confidently. Insufficient empathy affects her client interactions and teamwork, as she may fail to
recognize others’ needs or emotions, lowering trust and rapport. Collectively, these gaps hinder her
professional growth, as interpersonal effectiveness is crucial for leadership opportunities, team cohesion,
client satisfaction, and career advancement. Addressing these challenges is essential for Riya to excel
beyond technical competence and become a well-rounded professional.
(b) Suggest at least three practical strategies Riya can use to improve communication and active listening
within her team - Riya can improve communication and active listening using several strategies. First, she
should practice structured participation in meetings, preparing points in advance and gradually
contributing ideas to build confidence. Second, she should adopt active listening techniques, such as
maintaining eye contact, nodding, summarizing key points, and asking clarifying questions, which
demonstrates engagement and reduces misunderstandings. Third, Riya can seek feedback from colleagues
after discussions to understand how her communication is perceived and identify areas for improvement.
Additionally, using collaborative tools for idea-sharing can ensure her input is visible even if she hesitates
to speak in large meetings. Regular reflection on interactions, journaling communication challenges, and
observing effective communicators within her organization can further enhance her skills. Implementing
these strategies consistently will help Riya express herself confidently, understand team dynamics better,
and strengthen professional relationships.
(c) How can empathy and cultural sensitivity help Riya handle client interactions more effectively? -
Empathy allows Riya to understand clients’ perspectives, emotions, and expectations, enabling her to
respond appropriately and build trust. By actively listening and validating clients’ concerns, she can foster
a positive rapport and ensure solutions align with their needs. Cultural sensitivity further enhances
interactions, especially in a diverse client base. Being aware of cultural norms, communication styles, and
values helps Riya avoid misunderstandings, demonstrate respect, and adapt her approach accordingly. For
example, understanding a client’s preference for formal communication or decision-making processes can
prevent friction and miscommunication. By combining empathy with cultural awareness, Riya can
anticipate client concerns, address issues proactively, and strengthen long-term relationships, enhancing
client satisfaction and project outcomes. These skills not only improve professional credibility but also
contribute to her growth as a versatile, globally competent professional.
********** 6 **********
--- page 7 ---
(d) Imagine you are Riya’s mentor. Design a short action plan to enhance her teamwork, assertiveness,
and conflict-resolution skills. As Riya’s mentor, I would create a three-step action plan. First, to improve
teamwork, I would encourage her to actively participate in small group discussions, volunteer for
collaborative tasks, and acknowledge colleagues’ contributions to build mutual trust. Second, to enhance
assertiveness, she should practice expressing opinions clearly in low-stakes settings, gradually increasing
participation in larger meetings, and use “I” statements to communicate ideas confidently without
appearing aggressive. Third, for conflict resolution, I would teach her to listen empathetically, identify
the root cause of disagreements, and facilitate compromise by proposing solutions that respect all
perspectives. Additional steps include role-playing scenarios, seeking constructive feedback, and
reflecting on experiences to identify improvements. Regular check-ins would track progress, ensuring the
plan translates into consistent behavior changes. Implementing this action plan will strengthen Riya’s
interpersonal effectiveness, enabling her to collaborate, lead, and handle workplace challenges gracefully.
6.   Discuss the principles of effective leadership in promoting team collaboration, motivation, and engagement.
(a) Create a comprehensive plan showing how a leader can apply these principles to resolve conflicts, address
disengagement, and improve coordination within teams.
Synoptic Answer:
(a) Principles of Effective Leadership in promoting team collaboration, motivation, and engagement

sharing ideas.
Shared Vision and Goals: Aligning team efforts with common objectives increases motivation.
Empathy and Inclusivity: Recognizing individual differences promotes engagement.
Recognition and Feedback: Regular acknowledgment and constructive feedback boost morale.

Synoptic Answer:

1. Establish Clear, SMART Goals: Set specific, measurable, attainable, relevant, and time-bound

2. Promote Open Communication: Develop channels for transparent information sharing team meetings,

3. Promote Trust and Psychological Safety: Lead by example with honesty, integrity, and reliability.
Address conflicts proactively by listening actively to all parties involved.
4. Provide Vision and Purpose: Communicate a compelling vision that aligns with team values, inspiring

5. Build Inclusive and Respectful Culture: Encourage diversity, active listening, and mutual respect,

6. Recognize Contributions Regularly: Celebrate achievements and provide recognition tailored to

8. Encourage Collaboration and Innovation: Facilitate cross-functional teamwork and creative problem-
````

## 2025-26 · Re-Exam · Sem 5 · Answer key · paper c1bfee2c3ffd

- Departments: AI and Data Science
- Programmes: BTech AIDS 7056
- Source: `02_Question_Papers/2025/Sem_05/Re-Exam/BTech_AIDS_7056/Leading through Life Skills/ANS_Leading through Life Skills(702TG0I003)_2025-2026.pdf`
- Printed on paper: max marks: 100; duration hours: 3; exam date: 12 Feb 2026
- Text: text (8 pages)

````text
SYNOPTICS
SVKMs NMIMS
MUKESH PATEL SCH.OF TECH.MGMT ENGG-NM-M
Programme: BACHELOR OF TECHNOLOGY (MECHANICAL ENGINEERING) SIX
YEARS INTEGRATED PROGRAM/B. Tech (AI)/B. Tech (AI & DS)/B. Tech (AI & ML)/B.
Tech (CIVIL)/B. Tech (COMP)/B. Tech (CS)/B. Tech (CSE - CS)/B. Tech (DS)/B. Tech
(EXTC)/B. Tech Intg. (CE)/B. Tech Intg. (DS)/B. Tech Intg. (IT)/B. Tech Intg. (Mech)/B. Tech
(IT)/B. Tech (MECH)/B. Tech (MXTC)/MASTER OF BUSINESS ADMINISTRATION IN
TECHNOLOGY MANAGEMENT (COMPUTER ENGINEERING)/MASTER OF BUSINESS
ADMINISTRATION IN TECHNOLOGY MANAGEMENT (DATA SCIENCE)/MBA Tech
(AI)/MBA Tech (Comp)/MBA Tech (DS)/MBA Tech (IT)
Year: III / IV/Sem 5 Re-exam/Sem 7 Re-exam(Exam Year: 2025-2026)
Subject: Leading through Life Skills
Date: 12 Feb 2026   Time: 02:00 pm to 05:00 pm (03:00 Hrs.)
Max. Marks: 100
RE EXAMINATION (Acad. Year:2025-2026)
Instructions:
1. This question paper contains 8 pages.
2. 1. Candidates should read carefully the instructions.
3. 2. Answer to each new question to be started on a fresh page.
4. 3. Figures in brackets against each question indicate full marks.
5. 4. Question No.1 is compulsory. Answer any four questions from the rest questionnaire.
1. Short notes/definitions/concepts (All questions compulsory), (20 Marks)
I.   Explain the concept of “Righteousness (Dharma)” in decision-making using a one-line illustration
from any Indian scripture.
Synoptic Answer: Dharma = moral duty aligned with truth and justice; example; Krishna guiding Arjuna
toward action without attachment in the Bhagavad Gita.
II.   Discuss how empathy and active listening contribute to building trust in interpersonal relationships.
Provide real-life or workplace examples.
Synoptic Answer:
Empathy and active listening are crucial for building trust as they show understanding and respect for
others’ perspectives. Active listening involves fully focusing on the speaker, acknowledging their feelings,
and responding thoughtfully. Empathy allows one to emotionally connect and appreciate the other
person’s experiences. In the workplace, a manager who listens to an employee’s concerns about workload
and empathizes by adjusting responsibilities builds trust and loyalty. Similarly, in personal relationships,
empathizing with a friend’s challenges and validating their emotions strengthens bonds and fosters mutual
support. Together, these skills create an environment where people feel heard, valued, and understood,
which enhances collaboration, reduces misunderstandings, and promotes long-term trust.
III.   Explain manipulation in persuasion?
Synoptic Answer:
Manipulation in persuasion refers to influencing someone’s beliefs, emotions, or behavior through
deceptive or unethical means. While persuasion is a legitimate form of communication that aims for
mutual understanding and benefit, manipulation crosses the ethical line by exploiting another person’s
********** 1 **********
--- page 2 ---
weaknesses or emotions for personal gain. The manipulator often conceals true intentions, distorts facts,
or uses emotional pressure to lead the target toward a decision that serves the manipulator’s interests
rather than the individual’s own.
In manipulation, the communicator may present only selective information, exaggerate benefits, or use
fear, guilt, or flattery to influence the listener. For example, a salesperson may deliberately hide hidden
costs while emphasizing limited-time discounts, or a political campaign may play on citizens’ fears to
secure votes instead of presenting balanced facts. Unlike genuine persuasion, where the receiver is
encouraged to think critically and make an informed choice, manipulation seeks compliance through
control and psychological pressure.
The key difference lies in intent and transparency. Ethical persuasion respects autonomy—it seeks to
inform, inspire, and allow the listener to choose freely. Manipulation, however, undermines autonomy,
creating short-term agreement but long-term distrust. In professional and personal settings, manipulation
may bring temporary results, yet it damages credibility, relationships, and reputation. True persuasion,
therefore, must always be rooted in honesty, empathy, and integrity, ensuring that influence is exercised
responsibly and for mutual benefit.
IV. Value-Driven leadership.
Synoptic Answer:
Value-driven leadership is rooted in core ethical principles that guide decisions, behavior, and organizational
culture. Leaders act consistently with honesty, fairness, respect, and accountability. Such leadership ensures long-
term trust and sustainable success by aligning personal and organizational values. Example: Ratan Tata’s leadership
reflects integrity, social responsibility, and commitment to nation-building, values that shaped the Tata Group’s
legacy.
2. Case-based/scenario analysis & Essay/integrative reflection-application (Attempt Any 4), (80
Marks)
1.
(a) A student preparing for multiple internships feels anxious and distracted. Apply mindfulness and self-
motivation principles to design a two-week improvement plan.
Synoptic Answer:
Plan includes daily meditation, gratitude journaling, SMART goals, habit tracker; benefits, reduced
anxiety, improved focus, intrinsic motivation.
(b) Reflect on how body image influences self-esteem among young professionals in social-media-driven
cultures. Suggest two practices to nurture positive self-image.
Synoptic Answer:
Discuss self-concept distortion, comparison trap; propose cognitive reframing, digital detox, strengths
affirmation; relate to holistic wellbeing.
2.
(a) Discuss one example of how Authentic Leadership can be applied in a classroom or workplace setting.
Synoptic Answer: An example of authentic leadership applied in a classroom setting is when a teacher leads with
transparency and empathy by openly sharing their own challenges and learning experiences with students. This
approach builds trust and creates a safe environment where students feel valued and understood. The teacher listens
actively, encourages honest communication, and models integrity by aligning their words with actions. As a result,
students are more engaged, motivated, and willing to take risks in their learning because they see the teacher as
genuine and supportive. This authentic connection promotes collaboration and personal growth within the class.
********** 2 **********
--- page 3 ---
In a workplace setting, authentic leadership can be applied when a manager leads by example through
transparency and empathy. For instance, an authentic leader openly shares challenges they face, admits
mistakes, and invites feedback from team members. This transparency builds trust and encourages open
communication. The manager also genuinely cares for employees’ well-being, checking in on their
personal and professional needs and providing support during stressful times. By aligning words with
actions and showing vulnerability, the leader creates a positive, inclusive environment where employees
feel valued and motivated to contribute their best. This promotes trust, engagement, and a strong team
culture.
(b) Case Study: In a hospital, nurses report high stress and lack of emotional support from supervisors, affecting
patient care quality. Analyze how Value-driven Authentic Leadership can address stress and improve workplace
morale.
Synoptic Answer –
Value-driven Authentic Leadership can address nurse stress and improve workplace morale by promoting a
transparent, ethical, and supportive leadership culture rooted in core values and genuine relationships. It helps: -
Promotes Transparency and Trust: Authentic leaders openly communicate challenges and decisions,
building trust with nurses by showing honesty and integrity. This reduces uncertainty and anxiety that
contribute to stress.
Values-Driven Actions: By consistently aligning behavior with core values such as compassion, respect,
and patient-centered care, leaders create a meaningful work environment that resonates with nurses’
professional purpose, enhancing motivation and morale.
Emotional Support and Empathy: Authentic leaders demonstrate empathy and provide emotional support
by actively listening to nurses’ concerns and acknowledging their stress.
Encourages Self-Awareness and Reflection: Leaders who model self-awareness help nurses understand
and manage their own emotions better, which can reduce burnout and improve coping strategies.
Empowers and Engages Staff: Authentic leadership promotes empowerment by involving nurses in
decision-making and recognizing their contributions, which enhances job satisfaction and commitment.
Improves Workplace Culture: By modeling ethical behavior and consistency, authentic leaders cultivate
a positive and supportive culture where collaboration and mutual respect reduce interpersonal tensions.
In summary, Value-driven Authentic Leadership addresses high nurse stress by creating a transparent,
empathetic, and value-aligned environment that promotes trust, emotional support, employee engagement,
and ultimately improves patient care quality and workplace morale.
3.
(a) Compare traditional Indian philosophical concepts—Seva, Nishkama Karma, Satya, and Sahavāsa
with modern leadership practices. Illustrate your answer with examples linking each concept to
contemporary leadership behaviors.
Synoptic Answer:
Traditional Indian philosophical concepts provide a rich foundation for ethical and value-driven
leadership in modern organizations. Seva, or selfless service, emphasizes prioritizing the well-being and
growth of others without expectation of personal reward. In contemporary leadership, this aligns with
servant leadership, where leaders mentor and support their team members to achieve collective success.
For example, a manager who invests time in coaching juniors without seeking recognition embodies Seva
in practice.
Nishkama Karma, derived from the Bhagavad Gita, encourages performing duties with dedication while
remaining unattached to outcomes. Leaders guided by this principle focus on ethical and purposeful action
rather than solely on results or personal gain. A project lead who delivers high-quality work despite
********** 3 **********
--- page 4 ---
uncertain outcomes exemplifies this philosophy, fostering intrinsic motivation and resilience within the
team.
Satya, meaning truth and integrity, underlines honesty, transparency, and authenticity. Modern parallels
include ethical and authentic leadership, where credibility and trust are built through consistent adherence
to values. For instance, a CEO who openly communicates organizational challenges and makes decisions
aligned with ethical principles demonstrates Satya in action.
Finally, Sahavāsa emphasizes empathy, collaboration, and shared presence. It corresponds with
transformational or relational leadership, where leaders engage deeply with their teams to foster inclusion
and cohesion. A leader working alongside the team on challenging tasks, providing guidance and support,
is an example of Sahavāsa.
Together, these concepts guide leaders to act with integrity, empathy, selflessness, and commitment to
collective growth, showing that traditional Indian wisdom can effectively inform modern leadership
practices that prioritize people, ethics, and shared success.

(b) Case Study: A young project manager is leading a software development team for the first time. Team
members expect support, guidance, and clarity in task priorities.
Analyze how the manager can apply Servant Leadership principles in day-to-day practices to support the
team.
Synoptic Answer:
A young project manager leading a software development team for the first time can apply Servant
Leadership principles in day-to-day practices by focusing on serving the team’s needs first, providing
support, guidance, and clarity in task priorities to empower and enable the team to succeed. Key Practices
of Servant Leadership for the Manager: -
Active Listening: The manager should attentively listen to team members’ concerns, ideas, and feedback
without interruption.
Empathy: Understanding the perspectives and emotions of team members promotes a supportive
environment.
Clear Communication of Priorities: Servant leadership involves providing clarity in task priorities by
explaining the “why” behind project goals, enabling team members to align their efforts meaningfully.
********** 4 **********
--- page 5 ---
Removing Obstacles: The manager should proactively identify and eliminate barriers that prevent the
team from achieving their goals, such as resource constraints or unclear processes.
Empowerment and Trust: Encouraging self-organization and decision-making within the team promotes
ownership and motivation. The manager acts as a coach and mentor rather than an authoritarian figure.
Personal and Professional Development: Supporting team members’ growth through mentoring, training
opportunities, and recognizing their achievements helps build a stronger, more capable team.
Collaboration and Community: Building a sense of belonging and teamwork strengthens relationships
and drives collective success.
Balancing Service and Leadership: While serving the team, the manager must also guide, make decisions,
and hold the team accountable for outcomes, maintaining focus on project objectives.
By integrating these servant leadership behaviors, the young project manager creates an environment
where the team feels supported, prioritized, and motivated to excel, ultimately driving successful project
delivery and team satisfaction. This approach not only addresses immediate needs for support and clarity
but cultivates a culture of trust and continuous improvement.
4.   Case - When India’s Chandrayaan-2 mission lost contact with its lander moments before touchdown,
silence filled the ISRO control room. Years of work, sleepless nights, and national hopes seemed to
collapse in a single instant. Scientists sat frozen, their faces lit by the dim glow of monitors. In that heavy
silence, ISRO Chairman Dr. K. Sivan rose from his seat. He did not search for someone to blame. Instead,
he faced his team and said softly, “We did everything we could. This is not a failure—it is a step forward.”
His voice carried calm assurance, cutting through the tension in the room. He moved from desk to desk,
speaking personally to the engineers and young scientists who had worked under him, telling them how
proud he was of their dedication. Many were on the verge of tears; he reminded them that no mission is
wasted when it teaches something valuable. The next morning, as the world watched, Dr. Sivan took full
responsibility before the media, calling the outcome “a learning experience” and emphasizing the team’s
achievements in bringing the mission so close to success. He avoided technical blame or excuses, choosing
instead to focus on the team’s spirit and resilience.
Later, when Prime Minister Modi arrived to encourage the scientists, Dr. Sivan, overwhelmed with emotion, broke
down. It was a rare, human moment for a scientist known for his composure. Yet that vulnerability only
strengthened his team’s respect for him. They saw a leader who stood with them in failure, protected them in public,
and guided them back to confidence. In the months that followed, his words became the team’s motivation as they
began preparing for the next mission, proof that leadership is not about command, but about compassion and
courage in moments of loss. [CO-1, CO-2; Bloom’s Taxonomy, L4 (Analyze), L5 (Evaluate)]
(a) Analyze the servant leadership style demonstrated by Dr. K. Sivan in this case through key servant
leader behaviors, including emotional healing, putting followers first, commitment to their growth,
empathy, and accountability. (10 Marks)
(b) Analyze the value-driven leadership style demonstrated by Dr. K. Sivan in this case through core
values, integrity, Empathy, Accountability, Humility and resilience. (10 Marks)
Synoptic Answer:
(a)
Emotional Healing: Immediately after the lander lost contact, Dr. Sivan focused on alleviating the team’s
distress. By moving from desk to desk, speaking personally to each engineer, and acknowledging their
efforts, he helped them process their disappointment and regain composure. His reassurance that “no
mission is wasted when it teaches something valuable” provided emotional support that mitigated feelings
of failure and despair.
********** 5 **********
--- page 6 ---
Putting Followers First: Throughout the crisis, Dr. Sivan prioritized the well-being of his team over his
own public image or personal feelings. He shielded them from blame, publicly emphasizing their
achievements and dedication, and highlighted their collective contribution before taking personal
responsibility. This behavior demonstrated that the team’s morale and recognition were more important
than his own status.
Commitment to Growth: Dr. Sivan reframed the mission’s outcome as a learning experience, signaling
that mistakes are opportunities for improvement rather than reasons for reprimand. By encouraging
reflection and continued effort, he fostered a culture where scientists could grow professionally and
develop resilience, ensuring the organization benefited from both success and failure.
Empathy: His actions revealed deep empathy toward the team. Recognizing their emotional investment,
he validated their feelings, connected personally with those on the verge of tears, and openly displayed
his own vulnerability. This authenticity strengthened trust and reinforced the human dimension of
leadership.
Accountability: Dr. Sivan took full responsibility for the mission’s outcome in front of the media,
demonstrating ethical accountability. By owning the failure publicly while crediting the team’s efforts,
he modeled responsibility without scapegoating, reinforcing a culture of integrity and transparency.
(b) When the Chandrayaan-2 mission’s lander lost contact moments before touchdown, disappointment
swept through the ISRO control room. In that tense moment, Dr. K. Sivan did not retreat into technical
explanations or self-defense. Instead, he anchored his response in the core values that define ISRO’s
ethos; integrity, accountability, humility, empathy, and collective responsibility. He immediately took
responsibility for the outcome, emphasizing that the team had performed with utmost dedication. His
statement, “We did everything we could”, reflected integrity and transparency, showing the nation that
honest effort matters as much as results. Rather than assigning blame, he publicly acknowledged the
contribution of every scientist, from the senior engineers to the youngest interns. This act displayed
respect and equality, values deeply embedded in his leadership philosophy. Later, when he consoled team
members individually and even broke down emotionally, it revealed authenticity and compassion, values
that humanized leadership in a highly technical institution. His vulnerability did not weaken his authority;
it deepened trust, reinforcing the value of empathy in leadership.
In the following weeks, Dr. Sivan encouraged open discussions to analyze what went wrong, fostering a
culture of learning, honesty, and perseverance rather than fear or silence. By doing so, he embodied
accountability as a shared value, not a burden to be avoided.

5.
********** 6 **********
--- page 7 ---
(a) In a multicultural workplace, you notice cultural differences causing misunderstandings among
colleagues. How would you apply interpersonal skills such as active listening, cultural sensitivity, and
teamwork to create a collaborative and inclusive environment? (10 Marks)
Synoptic Answer:
I would start by actively listening to understand colleagues’ viewpoints and cultural contexts.
Demonstrating cultural sensitivity, I would avoid assumptions or judgments, recognizing that diverse
perspectives enrich problem-solving. Encouraging open dialogue, I would create opportunities for team
members to share ideas and express concerns comfortably. Promoting teamwork through inclusive
practices, such as rotating leadership roles or collaborative brainstorming sessions—ensures everyone
feels valued. Providing feedback respectfully and acknowledging contributions fosters mutual trust. By
applying empathy, patience, and awareness, I can reduce misunderstandings, enhance communication,
and build a cohesive, inclusive workplace where diversity strengthens collaboration and innovation.
(b) SparkFizz Beverages is a mid-sized soft drink company known for its affordable fruit-based sodas.
Over the last five years, the market has become increasingly competitive with the rise of health-conscious
consumers and eco-friendly startups. Sales of SparkFizz’s existing products have begun to decline,
especially among urban youth who prefer low-sugar or natural options. The company’s Managing Director
has set up a cross-functional innovation team led by Aarav, the Product Development Head, to create a
new beverage concept that can reposition SparkFizz as a youthful, health-oriented brand. Aarav wants the
team to use mind mapping as the starting tool for brainstorming and idea generation. He believes it can
help the group visually organize their thoughts, connect diverse insights, and identify unique opportunities
from different perspectives such as ingredients, packaging, sustainability, and marketing. As Aarav and
his team begin the product development process, design and justify how a mind map can be applied to
generate, connect, and refine innovative ideas for SparkFizz’s new health-oriented beverage.
Explain what key branches or categories you would include in the mind map and how this approach can
enhance creativity, collaboration, and clarity in decision-making. (10 marks)
Synoptic Answer:
A mind map can help SparkFizz’s team organize and expand their ideas visually while developing a new
health-oriented beverage. By starting with the main idea — “New Healthy Drink for SparkFizz”, the team
can branch out into key areas such as ingredients, target market, packaging, and marketing.
Under ingredients, they can explore natural options like fruit juices, herbs, or low-sugar alternatives. The
target audience branch can focus on health-conscious youth, working professionals, and fitness
enthusiasts. For packaging, the team might discuss eco-friendly bottles, recyclable materials, and
attractive label designs. In the marketing branch, they can add ideas like social media promotions, tie-ups
with gyms, or influencer campaigns. Other branches may include pricing, distribution channels, and future
product extensions like smoothies or hydration drinks.
Using a mind map allows the team to see connections between ideas, such as how natural ingredients link
with sustainability or how packaging supports brand image. It helps everyone contribute creatively, stay
organized, and build a complete product concept step by step.
In short, mind mapping encourages clear thinking, teamwork, and creativity, turning a simple idea into
an innovative, market-ready product.
6.   Rahul is a team leader in a software development company. Recently, one of his team members,
Anjali, has been underperforming and missing deadlines. Rahul notices that she seems stressed,
withdrawn, and less communicative during meetings. When he tries to discuss the issue, Anjali hesitates
to open up, fearing judgment or criticism. At the same time, other team members have started expressing
frustration due to delays in project deliverables. Rahul realizes that understanding Anjali’s perspective is
********** 7 **********
--- page 8 ---
crucial to resolving the situation effectively. He decides to practice empathy—actively listening to her
concerns, acknowledging her feelings, and offering support while balancing team productivity. Rahul also
reflects on how fostering a supportive and understanding environment can improve team morale, reduce
stress, and enhance overall performance.
(a) Identify the key areas where Rahul can demonstrate empathy. How can active listening, understanding,
and emotional support help him address Anjali’s challenges while maintaining team effectiveness? (10
Marks)
(b) Suggest practical steps Rahul can take to create an empathetic work environment that encourages open
communication, reduces stress, and motivates employees to perform at their best. (10 Marks)
Synoptic Answer:
(a) Identify the key areas where Rahul can demonstrate empathy. How can active listening, understanding,
and emotional support help him address Anjali’s challenges while maintaining team effectiveness?
Rahul can demonstrate empathy in several key areas: actively listening to Anjali’s concerns without
interrupting, acknowledging her feelings to validate her stress and frustrations, and offering emotional
support by providing guidance and encouragement. By creating a safe, non-judgmental space, he helps
Anjali feel understood and comfortable sharing the reasons behind her underperformance. Active listening
allows Rahul to identify specific obstacles—whether workload, personal issues, or skill gaps—that may
be affecting her productivity. Understanding her perspective enables him to tailor support, such as
adjusting deadlines or offering assistance, without compromising overall project timelines. Emotional
support fosters trust and confidence, motivating Anjali to re-engage with her tasks. By combining empathy
with constructive solutions, Rahul can maintain team effectiveness, reduce friction caused by delays, and
reinforce a collaborative environment that balances individual well-being with collective performance.
(b) Suggest practical steps Rahul can take to create an empathetic work environment that encourages
open communication, reduces stress, and motivates employees to perform at their best.
Rahul can implement several practical measures to cultivate an empathetic work culture. First, he should
schedule regular one-on-one check-ins with team members to understand their challenges and offer
support. Second, he can encourage open communication, ensuring employees feel safe sharing ideas,
concerns, or feedback without fear of judgment. Third, he should recognize efforts and achievements,
boosting morale and motivation. Providing flexible workload management, where responsibilities are
balanced according to capacity, helps reduce stress. Additionally, promoting peer support and
collaboration allows team members to assist each other, fostering trust. Rahul can also introduce stress-
relief initiatives, like short breaks, team-building activities, or wellness programs, to maintain overall
well-being. By consistently practicing these strategies, Rahul can build a supportive, empathetic
environment that improves employee engagement, enhances productivity, and strengthens team cohesion.
********** 8 **********
````

````text
1. Short notes/definitions/concepts (All questions compulsory), (20 Marks)
I.   Explain the concept of “Righteousness (Dharma)” in decision-making using a one-line illustration
from any Indian scripture.
Synoptic Answer: Dharma = moral duty aligned with truth and justice; example; Krishna guiding Arjuna
toward action without attachment in the Bhagavad Gita.
II.   Discuss how empathy and active listening contribute to building trust in interpersonal relationships.
Provide real-life or workplace examples.
Synoptic Answer:
Empathy and active listening are crucial for building trust as they show understanding and respect for
others’ perspectives. Active listening involves fully focusing on the speaker, acknowledging their feelings,
and responding thoughtfully. Empathy allows one to emotionally connect and appreciate the other
person’s experiences. In the workplace, a manager who listens to an employee’s concerns about workload
and empathizes by adjusting responsibilities builds trust and loyalty. Similarly, in personal relationships,
empathizing with a friend’s challenges and validating their emotions strengthens bonds and fosters mutual
support. Together, these skills create an environment where people feel heard, valued, and understood,
which enhances collaboration, reduces misunderstandings, and promotes long-term trust.
III.   Explain manipulation in persuasion?
Synoptic Answer:
Manipulation in persuasion refers to influencing someone’s beliefs, emotions, or behavior through
deceptive or unethical means. While persuasion is a legitimate form of communication that aims for
mutual understanding and benefit, manipulation crosses the ethical line by exploiting another person’s
weaknesses or emotions for personal gain. The manipulator often conceals true intentions, distorts facts,
or uses emotional pressure to lead the target toward a decision that serves the manipulator’s interests
rather than the individual’s own.
********** 1 **********
--- page 2 ---
In manipulation, the communicator may present only selective information, exaggerate benefits, or use
fear, guilt, or flattery to influence the listener. For example, a salesperson may deliberately hide hidden
costs while emphasizing limited-time discounts, or a political campaign may play on citizens’ fears to
secure votes instead of presenting balanced facts. Unlike genuine persuasion, where the receiver is
encouraged to think critically and make an informed choice, manipulation seeks compliance through
control and psychological pressure.
The key difference lies in intent and transparency. Ethical persuasion respects autonomy—it seeks to
inform, inspire, and allow the listener to choose freely. Manipulation, however, undermines autonomy,
creating short-term agreement but long-term distrust. In professional and personal settings, manipulation
may bring temporary results, yet it damages credibility, relationships, and reputation. True persuasion,
therefore, must always be rooted in honesty, empathy, and integrity, ensuring that influence is exercised
responsibly and for mutual benefit.
IV. Value-Driven leadership.
Synoptic Answer:
Value-driven leadership is rooted in core ethical principles that guide decisions, behavior, and organizational
culture. Leaders act consistently with honesty, fairness, respect, and accountability. Such leadership ensures long-
term trust and sustainable success by aligning personal and organizational values. Example: Ratan Tata’s leadership
reflects integrity, social responsibility, and commitment to nation-building, values that shaped the Tata Group’s
legacy.

existence)   Leadership   inclusion and cohesion
connectedness
(b) Case Study: A young project manager is leading a software development team for the first time. Team
members expect support, guidance, and clarity in task priorities.
Analyze how the manager can apply Servant Leadership principles in day-to-day practices to support the
team.
Synoptic Answer:
A young project manager leading a software development team for the first time can apply Servant
Leadership principles in day-to-day practices by focusing on serving the team’s needs first, providing
support, guidance, and clarity in task priorities to empower and enable the team to succeed. Key Practices
of Servant Leadership for the Manager: -
Active Listening: The manager should attentively listen to team members’ concerns, ideas, and feedback
without interruption.
Empathy: Understanding the perspectives and emotions of team members promotes a supportive
environment.
Clear Communication of Priorities: Servant leadership involves providing clarity in task priorities by
explaining the “why” behind project goals, enabling team members to align their efforts meaningfully.
Removing Obstacles: The manager should proactively identify and eliminate barriers that prevent the
team from achieving their goals, such as resource constraints or unclear processes.
Empowerment and Trust: Encouraging self-organization and decision-making within the team promotes
ownership and motivation. The manager acts as a coach and mentor rather than an authoritarian figure.
********** 4 **********
--- page 5 ---
Personal and Professional Development: Supporting team members’ growth through mentoring, training
opportunities, and recognizing their achievements helps build a stronger, more capable team.
Collaboration and Community: Building a sense of belonging and teamwork strengthens relationships
and drives collective success.
Balancing Service and Leadership: While serving the team, the manager must also guide, make decisions,
and hold the team accountable for outcomes, maintaining focus on project objectives.
By integrating these servant leadership behaviors, the young project manager creates an environment
where the team feels supported, prioritized, and motivated to excel, ultimately driving successful project
delivery and team satisfaction. This approach not only addresses immediate needs for support and clarity
but cultivates a culture of trust and continuous improvement.
4.   Case - When India’s Chandrayaan-2 mission lost contact with its lander moments before touchdown,
silence filled the ISRO control room. Years of work, sleepless nights, and national hopes seemed to
collapse in a single instant. Scientists sat frozen, their faces lit by the dim glow of monitors. In that heavy
silence, ISRO Chairman Dr. K. Sivan rose from his seat. He did not search for someone to blame. Instead,
he faced his team and said softly, “We did everything we could. This is not a failure—it is a step forward.”
His voice carried calm assurance, cutting through the tension in the room. He moved from desk to desk,
speaking personally to the engineers and young scientists who had worked under him, telling them how
proud he was of their dedication. Many were on the verge of tears; he reminded them that no mission is
wasted when it teaches something valuable. The next morning, as the world watched, Dr. Sivan took full
responsibility before the media, calling the outcome “a learning experience” and emphasizing the team’s
achievements in bringing the mission so close to success. He avoided technical blame or excuses, choosing
instead to focus on the team’s spirit and resilience.
Later, when Prime Minister Modi arrived to encourage the scientists, Dr. Sivan, overwhelmed with emotion, broke
down. It was a rare, human moment for a scientist known for his composure. Yet that vulnerability only
strengthened his team’s respect for him. They saw a leader who stood with them in failure, protected them in public,
and guided them back to confidence. In the months that followed, his words became the team’s motivation as they
began preparing for the next mission, proof that leadership is not about command, but about compassion and
courage in moments of loss. [CO-1, CO-2; Bloom’s Taxonomy, L4 (Analyze), L5 (Evaluate)]
(a) Analyze the servant leadership style demonstrated by Dr. K. Sivan in this case through key servant
leader behaviors, including emotional healing, putting followers first, commitment to their growth,
empathy, and accountability. (10 Marks)
(b) Analyze the value-driven leadership style demonstrated by Dr. K. Sivan in this case through core
values, integrity, Empathy, Accountability, Humility and resilience. (10 Marks)
Synoptic Answer:
(a)
Emotional Healing: Immediately after the lander lost contact, Dr. Sivan focused on alleviating the team’s
distress. By moving from desk to desk, speaking personally to each engineer, and acknowledging their
efforts, he helped them process their disappointment and regain composure. His reassurance that “no
mission is wasted when it teaches something valuable” provided emotional support that mitigated feelings
of failure and despair.
Putting Followers First: Throughout the crisis, Dr. Sivan prioritized the well-being of his team over his
own public image or personal feelings. He shielded them from blame, publicly emphasizing their
achievements and dedication, and highlighted their collective contribution before taking personal
responsibility. This behavior demonstrated that the team’s morale and recognition were more important
than his own status.
********** 5 **********
--- page 6 ---
Commitment to Growth: Dr. Sivan reframed the mission’s outcome as a learning experience, signaling
that mistakes are opportunities for improvement rather than reasons for reprimand. By encouraging
reflection and continued effort, he fostered a culture where scientists could grow professionally and
develop resilience, ensuring the organization benefited from both success and failure.
Empathy: His actions revealed deep empathy toward the team. Recognizing their emotional investment,
he validated their feelings, connected personally with those on the verge of tears, and openly displayed
his own vulnerability. This authenticity strengthened trust and reinforced the human dimension of
leadership.
Accountability: Dr. Sivan took full responsibility for the mission’s outcome in front of the media,
demonstrating ethical accountability. By owning the failure publicly while crediting the team’s efforts,
he modeled responsibility without scapegoating, reinforcing a culture of integrity and transparency.
(b) When the Chandrayaan-2 mission’s lander lost contact moments before touchdown, disappointment
swept through the ISRO control room. In that tense moment, Dr. K. Sivan did not retreat into technical
explanations or self-defense. Instead, he anchored his response in the core values that define ISRO’s
ethos; integrity, accountability, humility, empathy, and collective responsibility. He immediately took
responsibility for the outcome, emphasizing that the team had performed with utmost dedication. His
statement, “We did everything we could”, reflected integrity and transparency, showing the nation that
honest effort matters as much as results. Rather than assigning blame, he publicly acknowledged the
contribution of every scientist, from the senior engineers to the youngest interns. This act displayed
respect and equality, values deeply embedded in his leadership philosophy. Later, when he consoled team
members individually and even broke down emotionally, it revealed authenticity and compassion, values
that humanized leadership in a highly technical institution. His vulnerability did not weaken his authority;
it deepened trust, reinforcing the value of empathy in leadership.
In the following weeks, Dr. Sivan encouraged open discussions to analyze what went wrong, fostering a
culture of learning, honesty, and perseverance rather than fear or silence. By doing so, he embodied
accountability as a shared value, not a burden to be avoided.

5.
(a) In a multicultural workplace, you notice cultural differences causing misunderstandings among
colleagues. How would you apply interpersonal skills such as active listening, cultural sensitivity, and
teamwork to create a collaborative and inclusive environment? (10 Marks)
Synoptic Answer:
I would start by actively listening to understand colleagues’ viewpoints and cultural contexts.
Demonstrating cultural sensitivity, I would avoid assumptions or judgments, recognizing that diverse
********** 6 **********
--- page 7 ---
perspectives enrich problem-solving. Encouraging open dialogue, I would create opportunities for team
members to share ideas and express concerns comfortably. Promoting teamwork through inclusive
practices, such as rotating leadership roles or collaborative brainstorming sessions—ensures everyone
feels valued. Providing feedback respectfully and acknowledging contributions fosters mutual trust. By
applying empathy, patience, and awareness, I can reduce misunderstandings, enhance communication,
and build a cohesive, inclusive workplace where diversity strengthens collaboration and innovation.
(b) SparkFizz Beverages is a mid-sized soft drink company known for its affordable fruit-based sodas.
Over the last five years, the market has become increasingly competitive with the rise of health-conscious
consumers and eco-friendly startups. Sales of SparkFizz’s existing products have begun to decline,
especially among urban youth who prefer low-sugar or natural options. The company’s Managing Director
has set up a cross-functional innovation team led by Aarav, the Product Development Head, to create a
new beverage concept that can reposition SparkFizz as a youthful, health-oriented brand. Aarav wants the
team to use mind mapping as the starting tool for brainstorming and idea generation. He believes it can
help the group visually organize their thoughts, connect diverse insights, and identify unique opportunities
from different perspectives such as ingredients, packaging, sustainability, and marketing. As Aarav and
his team begin the product development process, design and justify how a mind map can be applied to
generate, connect, and refine innovative ideas for SparkFizz’s new health-oriented beverage.
Explain what key branches or categories you would include in the mind map and how this approach can
enhance creativity, collaboration, and clarity in decision-making. (10 marks)
Synoptic Answer:
A mind map can help SparkFizz’s team organize and expand their ideas visually while developing a new
health-oriented beverage. By starting with the main idea — “New Healthy Drink for SparkFizz”, the team
can branch out into key areas such as ingredients, target market, packaging, and marketing.
Under ingredients, they can explore natural options like fruit juices, herbs, or low-sugar alternatives. The
target audience branch can focus on health-conscious youth, working professionals, and fitness
enthusiasts. For packaging, the team might discuss eco-friendly bottles, recyclable materials, and
attractive label designs. In the marketing branch, they can add ideas like social media promotions, tie-ups
with gyms, or influencer campaigns. Other branches may include pricing, distribution channels, and future
product extensions like smoothies or hydration drinks.
Using a mind map allows the team to see connections between ideas, such as how natural ingredients link
with sustainability or how packaging supports brand image. It helps everyone contribute creatively, stay
organized, and build a complete product concept step by step.
In short, mind mapping encourages clear thinking, teamwork, and creativity, turning a simple idea into
an innovative, market-ready product.
6.   Rahul is a team leader in a software development company. Recently, one of his team members,
Anjali, has been underperforming and missing deadlines. Rahul notices that she seems stressed,
withdrawn, and less communicative during meetings. When he tries to discuss the issue, Anjali hesitates
to open up, fearing judgment or criticism. At the same time, other team members have started expressing
frustration due to delays in project deliverables. Rahul realizes that understanding Anjali’s perspective is
crucial to resolving the situation effectively. He decides to practice empathy—actively listening to her
concerns, acknowledging her feelings, and offering support while balancing team productivity. Rahul also
reflects on how fostering a supportive and understanding environment can improve team morale, reduce
stress, and enhance overall performance.
********** 7 **********
--- page 8 ---
(a) Identify the key areas where Rahul can demonstrate empathy. How can active listening, understanding,
and emotional support help him address Anjali’s challenges while maintaining team effectiveness? (10
Marks)
(b) Suggest practical steps Rahul can take to create an empathetic work environment that encourages open
communication, reduces stress, and motivates employees to perform at their best. (10 Marks)
Synoptic Answer:
(a) Identify the key areas where Rahul can demonstrate empathy. How can active listening, understanding,
and emotional support help him address Anjali’s challenges while maintaining team effectiveness?
Rahul can demonstrate empathy in several key areas: actively listening to Anjali’s concerns without
interrupting, acknowledging her feelings to validate her stress and frustrations, and offering emotional
support by providing guidance and encouragement. By creating a safe, non-judgmental space, he helps
Anjali feel understood and comfortable sharing the reasons behind her underperformance. Active listening
allows Rahul to identify specific obstacles—whether workload, personal issues, or skill gaps—that may
be affecting her productivity. Understanding her perspective enables him to tailor support, such as
adjusting deadlines or offering assistance, without compromising overall project timelines. Emotional
support fosters trust and confidence, motivating Anjali to re-engage with her tasks. By combining empathy
with constructive solutions, Rahul can maintain team effectiveness, reduce friction caused by delays, and
reinforce a collaborative environment that balances individual well-being with collective performance.
(b) Suggest practical steps Rahul can take to create an empathetic work environment that encourages
open communication, reduces stress, and motivates employees to perform at their best.
Rahul can implement several practical measures to cultivate an empathetic work culture. First, he should
schedule regular one-on-one check-ins with team members to understand their challenges and offer
support. Second, he can encourage open communication, ensuring employees feel safe sharing ideas,
concerns, or feedback without fear of judgment. Third, he should recognize efforts and achievements,
boosting morale and motivation. Providing flexible workload management, where responsibilities are
balanced according to capacity, helps reduce stress. Additionally, promoting peer support and
collaboration allows team members to assist each other, fostering trust. Rahul can also introduce stress-
relief initiatives, like short breaks, team-building activities, or wellness programs, to maintain overall
well-being. By consistently practicing these strategies, Rahul can build a supportive, empathetic
environment that improves employee engagement, enhances productivity, and strengthens team cohesion.
********** 8 **********
````
